# Foundational Conversation Audit

**Status:** Active working memory; non-authoritative
**Intended destination:** Inputs may later inform `01-philosophy/`, `02-governance/`, `03-canon/`, or `04-academy/` after explicit review and acceptance
**Owner and final reviewer:** Sheldon Phillips
**Audit role:** Codex
**Started:** 2026-07-21

## Purpose

Maintain a durable, local audit of material Sheldon transfers from the foundational ChatGPT conversation while:

- preserving each submitted response faithfully
- organizing the emerging concepts and proposed documents
- checking alignment with the existing Spark OS authority model
- identifying duplication, over-complication, premature abstraction, and feature creep
- preparing a later installation prompt without treating conversation content as accepted repository knowledge

This file is working memory, not a source of truth. No item becomes Philosophy, Governance, Canon, or Academy content until Sheldon explicitly accepts its destination and instructs installation.

## Operating Method

For each response Sheldon posts in this Codex task:

1. Add the original text to the Source Ledger with a stable entry number.
2. Update the Conversation Structure only where the response adds or changes substance.
3. Record proposed documents without creating them prematurely.
4. Add concise audit findings and classify their urgency.
5. Preserve unresolved questions and contradictions rather than silently resolving them.
6. Keep parked ideas visible without allowing them to expand the active scope.
7. Revisit every live question and concern against each new exchange, recording whether the evidence strengthens, weakens, resolves, or reframes it.
8. Update the Live Provisional Glossary with new terms, changed meanings, important distinctions, unresolved ambiguity, and likely canonical ownership.
9. For each Values boundary test, record the underlying Value signal before downstream allocation, the cost or refusal point that makes it meaningful, any candidate Principle, likely implementation owner, and whether the test strengthens, weakens, refines, merges, or rejects the candidate.

### Audit Labels

- **Clear:** coherent and appropriately scoped
- **Watch:** useful, but at risk of duplication, ambiguity, or premature detail
- **Drift:** departs from the stated foundational objective
- **Conflict:** contradicts another proposal or an active Spark OS authority
- **Decision needed:** requires Sheldon's explicit judgment

## Current Objective

Develop and review the foundational documents for Spark OS, beginning with the Philosophy foundation already identified in `brain/current-state.md`, while preventing the conversation from expanding into domain-software architecture or unnecessary repository machinery.

## Scope Guardrails

### In Scope

- foundational beliefs, values, principles, mental models, and shared language
- the purpose and boundaries of each proposed foundational document
- relationships and dependencies among those documents
- decisions required before material can enter the repository
- an eventual installation prompt and placement plan

### Out of Scope Unless Explicitly Reopened

- domain-software architecture
- implementation features and product specifications
- new repository control planes, memory systems, or acceptance layers
- Academy content before its source Philosophy or Canon is accepted
- treating polished language as evidence that a concept has been approved

## Conversation Structure

### Active Thread

The coordinated foundational installation is independently verified. The approved Manifesto and Values, governing work method, navigation, and repository integrity pass; Principles remain the next Philosophy development phase when Sheldon authorizes it.

### Emerging Concepts

- Conversation content is exploratory until explicitly accepted into the repository.
- The audit should protect coherence and simplicity, not become another authority layer.
- Source preservation and interpretive synthesis must remain visibly separate.
- The existing Spark Manifesto is primarily organizational: it concerns the brokerage, advisor development, client care, professional standards, and legacy.
- A dependency-oriented reasoning pattern is visible in the Manifesto and may be a distinctive Spark OS philosophical thread.
- The browser conversation proposes that knowledge should become understanding, understanding should become competence, and competence should support sound judgment and trustworthy action.
- Technology is treated as an implementation in service of human and organizational capability, not as the purpose of Spark OS.
- Exploratory granularity and canonical simplicity serve different readers and should not be confused.
- Concrete exploration can precede abstraction without requiring every explored concept to become a separate repository file.
- Elegance is not evidence of foundational status; repeated survival through exploration, challenge, and application is a stronger signal.
- Continuous mapping can preserve architectural direction during exploration, provided it does not create duplicate ownership across the five destination documents.
- Values become governing commitments when Spark is willing to bear meaningful cost rather than compromise them.
- Application is evidence of competence or usefulness, not by itself proof that an underlying proposition is true.
- The repository may preserve authoritative knowledge representations without guaranteeing that a person understands or can competently apply them.
- Precise conceptual boundaries enable coherent Philosophy, but ontology is one supporting strand rather than the whole purpose of Philosophy.
- External knowledge should be preferred over unnecessary reinvention when it is relevant, authoritative, current, and critically evaluated.
- Knowledge, Understanding, Judgment, and Competence are related but do not form a universal ladder or guarantee one another.
- Repository authority, epistemic confidence, and temporal currency are different properties and should not be collapsed into one status list.
- Spark OS is being developed for Spark Mortgages; broader organizational applicability may emerge later but should not be assumed or abstracted prematurely.
- Closing an exploration means it is fit to serve as source material, not that its contents have become canonical.

### Proposed Document Set

The browser conversation proposes eleven Philosophy documents:

1. Manifesto
2. Purpose
3. Epistemology
4. First Principles
5. Beliefs
6. Values
7. Design Principles
8. Mental Models
9. Decision Framework
10. AI Collaboration
11. Continuous Improvement

This proposal is not accepted. It conflicts with the active five-document framework and contains several likely overlaps. The current low-complexity mapping to evaluate is:

| Proposed material | Existing likely home | Audit view |
|---|---|---|
| Purpose | `manifesto.md` | Direct fit; a separate chapter is probably unnecessary. |
| Epistemology | Manifesto, Principles, Mental Models, and Glossary | Important theme, but a standalone authority has not been justified. |
| First Principles / Design Principles | `principles.md` | Likely duplicate categories unless a durable distinction is proven. |
| Beliefs | Manifesto or `values.md` | Risks becoming an intermediate layer with no unique job. |
| Values | `values.md` | Direct fit. |
| Mental Models / Decision Framework | `mental-models.md` and `principles.md` | Likely separable as content, not necessarily as files. |
| AI Collaboration | `02-governance/ai-governance/` | Primarily governance; philosophical commitments can be linked from Philosophy. |
| Continuous Improvement | Values, Principles, or Mental Models | Cross-cutting theme; a separate document risks fragmentation. |
| Organizational Spark Manifesto | Destination undecided | It does not cleanly fit the current Spark OS Manifesto boundary, but no new organizational repository area exists yet. |
| Order | `principles.md` or `mental-models.md` | A possible dependency principle/model; not yet established as a universal truth or a separate paper requirement. |
| Knowledge / Understanding | `manifesto.md`, `mental-models.md`, and `glossary.md` | Definitions and transformations should be developed together enough to avoid circularity. |
| Competence | `values.md`, `principles.md`, and `glossary.md` | Strongly grounded in the organizational Manifesto, but its precise philosophical role remains to be defined. |
| Trust | `values.md`, `principles.md`, and `glossary.md` | Likely an earned outcome and protected commitment; those roles must be distinguished. |

### Dependencies

- Existing Philosophy document boundaries in `01-philosophy/README.md`
- Repository authority and contribution rules
- Sheldon's explicit acceptance of substantive decisions

### Open Questions

- Should Spark OS remain specifically the knowledge operating system for Spark Mortgages, or become a general operating philosophy intended to produce many kinds of organizations?
- Does the organizational Spark Manifesto belong in this repository, and if so under which existing authority? Creating a new `Spark/` area would be an architectural decision, not routine placement.
- Should the Spark OS Manifesto remain the first Philosophy document as currently governed, or should it become a concluding document? The latter reverses an active repository dependency.
- Can the valuable epistemological material be expressed inside the five existing Philosophy documents before considering a new file?
- Which dependency statements are accepted Spark beliefs, and which are browser-chat extrapolations?
- Is the supplied “Purpose” prose a candidate Spark OS Manifesto draft, or merely source material for it?

## Values Exploration 001 Audit

**Phase:** Audit
**Goal:** Determine the smallest credible initial set of distinct, costly, durable commitments.
**Constraint:** Audit candidates before drafting the Values document.

### Candidate Set

| Candidate family | Provisional commitment | Demonstrated cost or refusal point | Primary overlap risk | Audit status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Faithfulness to material reality | Spark will not distort material facts, uncertainty, performance, responsibility, authority, or history for convenience, appearance, speed, or profit, and will not ignore credible evidence it should reasonably examine. | Lost revenue, slower action, uncomfortable reporting, verification effort, correction cost, and reputational discomfort. | Plain-language name may sound selectively honest; commitment must preserve materiality and reasonable evidence. | Set-compared — Distill independently |
| Client interest before transaction | Spark will not compromise truthful, competent advice serving the client's informed interests to secure or preserve a transaction. | Lost commission, declined business, delay, referral to another institution, conflict with partners, and no future client benefit. | Plain-language title must preserve professional boundaries and avoid unrestricted client preference. | Set-compared — Distill independently |
| Responsibility cannot be outsourced | Spark will keep accountability explicitly and proportionately owned when work, authority, decisions, or execution are delegated, automated, shared, or externally supplied. | Slower automation, added review, explicit ownership, correction duties, accepting organizational fault, and refusal of convenient blame transfer. | Plain-language name must carry identifiable ownership and fair proportionality without banning delegation. | Set-compared — Distill independently |
| Capability before scale | Spark will not knowingly pursue or preserve growth that predictably and materially degrades its continuing ability to serve and operate responsibly. | Foregone volume and revenue, delayed expansion, infrastructure investment, exposed weakness, narrower scope, and temporary repair cost. | `Fragile` must distinguish structural weakness from temporary difficulty, learning, and responsible ambition. | Set-compared — Distill independently |
| Stewardship across time | Spark will accept proportionate present cost so future Spark people do not inherit avoidable ignorance, unexplained systems, preventable relearning, or unnecessary loss of consequential knowledge. | Documentation, maintenance, succession, migration, review, preservation without immediate benefit, and responsible retirement or deletion despite familiarity. | Plain-language name must bound the beneficiary and preserve responsible retirement alongside continuity. | Set-compared — Distill independently |

### Required Candidate Card

Every candidate will be audited using the active seven-part repository structure:

1. **Name** — memorable and precise
2. **Commitment** — what Spark refuses to compromise
3. **Meaning** — what the commitment means in Spark Mortgages
4. **Observable behavior** — evidence that Spark is living it
5. **Trade-off** — the meaningful cost Spark will bear
6. **Failure mode** — how the Value can be weakened, gamed, overextended, or misunderstood
7. **Derived Principles** — candidate decision rules, without drafting their full implementation

Each candidate must also receive an Audit disposition: **retain, refine, merge, reject, or reopen**.

### Audit Sequence

1. Complete one candidate card at a time, starting with Faithfulness to Material Reality.
2. Test each card against the repository's Value acceptance questions.
3. Compare the completed cards as a set for overlap, gaps, conflict, generic virtue language, and excessive scope.
4. Record one primary owner for every derivative concept.
5. Close only the candidates fit to serve as source material.
6. Distill the smallest credible Values document.
7. Audit the document as a whole before requesting Sheldon's approval.

### Candidate Audit 01 — Faithfulness to Material Reality

**Browser disposition:** Retain
**Independent audit disposition:** Refine

**Substantive result:** The commitment has earned continued inclusion. It is costly, durable, observable, and distinct from client interest. It survived uncertainty, privacy, confidentiality, simplification, forecasts, disputed claims, corrections, internal reporting, metric gaming, and lawful disclosure limits.

**Required refinements before Distill:**

- Test a more memorable plain-language name such as `Truth Over Appearance` or `Represent Reality Honestly` without losing materiality and uncertainty.
- Replace a knowledge standard limited to intentional distortion with responsibility for reasonable verification and credible contrary evidence.
- Avoid turning “material reality” into a claim of perfect objectivity or exhaustive disclosure.
- Keep observable behaviors concise in the eventual Values document; the full audit evidence remains in the Workbench.
- Limit Derived Principles to named candidates and assign detailed controls to Governance during later allocation.

**Set-comparison question:** Can the Value protect honest internal and external representation while Candidate 02 independently protects whose interests client advice serves?

### Candidate Audit 02 — Client Interest Before Transaction

**Browser disposition:** Refine
**Independent audit disposition:** Refine

**Substantive result:** The commitment qualifies as a distinct Value. It remains binding without repeat business, referrals, reputation benefit, or revenue. It protects informed client interests and autonomy while preserving professional, legal, regulatory, contractual, confidentiality, authority, and feasibility boundaries.

**Required refinements before Distill:**

- Test whether `Client Interest Before Transaction` is the most precise memorable name; avoid implying that every client preference overrides professional responsibility.
- Define equal treatment as equal honesty, competence, care, and respect within clearly communicated service boundaries—not identical time, scope, or effort in every engagement.
- Limit external-option expectations to material alternatives Spark knows or should reasonably identify within its competence and agreed service scope.
- Verify later use of `suitable`, `client interest`, and related professional language against applicable mortgage requirements before canonical installation.
- Condense the eventual entry substantially and allocate documentation, escalation, conflict-control, and referral mechanics downstream.

**Set-comparison question:** Can Candidate 02 protect loyalty to the client's informed interests without duplicating Candidate 01's truthfulness or Candidate 03's accountability?

### Candidate 03 Pre-Audit Framing — Responsibility Cannot Be Outsourced

The browser response correctly distinguishes legitimate delegation from disappearance of accountability. Work, authority, responsibility, and bounded decisions may be allocated or delegated; automation and vendors may perform work; shared bodies may decide. The Value signal is that accountability must remain explicit before, during, and after those arrangements.

Two boundary cases must anchor the formal card:

- **Success without ownership:** an automated process can violate the candidate before any visible failure if continued success has allowed its owner, limits, monitoring duty, and escalation path to disappear.
- **Responsibility without authority:** Spark should not assign an individual accountability for systemic conditions they lack meaningful authority or ability to influence. Individual and organizational accountability must be distinguished and proportionate to role, authority, knowledge, and control.

**Audit caution:** `Responsibility Cannot Be Outsourced` is memorable but overbroad. It can imply that delegation is itself improper or that every person remains personally responsible for everything. The formal audit should test names such as `Accountability Must Have an Owner` or `Responsibility Must Remain Explicit` without adopting either before the full card and complete-set comparison.

**Required next action:** Build and test the seven-part Candidate 03 card. Do not assign retain, refine, merge, reject, or reopen until that audit is complete.

### Candidate Audit 03 — Responsibility Cannot Be Outsourced

**Browser disposition:** Refine
**Independent audit disposition:** Refine

**Substantive result:** Candidate 03 qualifies as a separate Value. It imposes real cost, constrains actual organizational choices, survives legitimate delegation and reasonable reliance, and adds a cost-bearing refusal beyond the Manifesto: Spark will not permit accountability to disappear or become unfairly detached from authority merely because explicit ownership, oversight, or organizational responsibility is inconvenient.

**Required refinements before Distill:**

- Replace or refine the provisional name so it does not imply that work, bounded authority, assigned responsibility, or vendor duties cannot be legitimately delegated.
- Preserve three concise commitments: material accountability remains explicit; accountability is proportionate to role, authority, knowledge, influence, and reasonable opportunity to act; Spark remains accountable for material operating conditions it creates or approves.
- Avoid treating proportionality as a mathematical allocation of blame. Different parties may hold simultaneous, non-zero-sum duties, and applicable law or professional obligations may assign accountability independently.
- Keep `reasonable reliance` bounded by role, approval, training, material warning signs, and escalation duties without requiring universal re-verification.
- Condense ten Derived Principles. Several are implementation controls for Governance, AI Governance, vendor management, incident review, and organizational design rather than independent Philosophy Principles.
- Keep detailed responsibility matrices, approval chains, monitoring rules, recourse mechanics, and vendor controls out of the eventual Values entry.

**Set-comparison question:** Does Candidate 03 protect explicit and fair accountability without absorbing Candidate 01's honest representation duties, Candidate 04's organizational capability constraints, or Candidate 05's stewardship duties?

### Candidate Audit 04 — Capability Before Scale

**Browser disposition:** Refine
**Independent audit disposition:** Refine

**Substantive result:** The candidate qualifies only after separating the Value-level refusal from capability as an organizational condition. Capability is useful but morally neutral. The distinct costly commitment is that Spark will not knowingly pursue or preserve growth whose predictable, material operating consequences destroy or degrade its continuing ability to serve clients and meet its responsibilities.

**Required refinements before Distill:**

- Replace or refine `Capability Before Scale`; capability and scale can develop together, so `before` incorrectly suggests a completed-capability prerequisite.
- Frame the Value around refusal of predictably destructive or structurally fragile growth, not a general preference for capability, quality, sustainability, caution, or comfortable workloads.
- Preserve responsible ambition, bounded experimentation, sufficient rather than perfect readiness, temporary supported strain, honest mistakes, and learning through growth.
- Keep capability directed and constrained by truthful representation, client interest, accountability, law, regulation, and external obligations.
- Do not reduce capability to production, low volume, few reported errors, employee silence, or a composite score. Indicators require context and can be gamed.
- Condense nine Derived Principles and allocate capacity measures, staffing, workload, resilience, experimentation, maintenance, and growth-review mechanics downstream.

**Set-comparison question:** Is Candidate 04's refusal of structurally degrading growth distinct from Candidate 05's duty to preserve future capability and institutional knowledge, or are both expressions of one longer-term stewardship commitment?

### Candidate Audit 05 — Stewardship Across Time

**Browser disposition:** Refine
**Independent audit disposition:** Refine

**Substantive result:** Candidate 05 contains a plausible independent Value: Spark accepts proportionate present cost for future Spark people who cannot participate in today's preservation, succession, or retirement decisions. The commitment is not to preserve information as an end; it is to avoid imposing preventable ignorance, unexplained inheritance, repeated consequential mistakes, and unnecessary relearning solely because continuity offered no immediate payoff.

**Required refinements before Distill:**

- Keep the beneficiary bounded to future Spark people and clients materially affected by organizational continuity; avoid an undefined obligation to all future people.
- Test a more memorable and narrower name. `Stewardship Across Time` can absorb clients, resources, culture, capability, and general organizational stewardship.
- Preserve selective continuity and responsible retirement together. The Value must not imply exhaustive capture, permanent authority, or preference for inherited knowledge over fresh investigation.
- Distinguish the commitment from its mechanisms: Governance owns authority, lifecycle, retention, security, and deletion; Academy supports transmission; Canon preserves accepted guidance; repository architecture supports storage and retrieval.
- Do not promise future usefulness or freedom from relearning. The refusal concerns reasonably foreseeable, material, and avoidable loss assessed proportionately at the time.
- Consolidate ten Derived Principles and route records management, privacy, security, succession, metadata, provenance, archival, and maintenance controls downstream.

**Set-comparison question:** Does responsibility to absent future beneficiaries justify an independent Value, or can Candidates 03 and 04 protect the same refusal without reducing future people to an operational-capability rationale?

### Complete-Set Comparison

**Browser disposition:** Ready to Close and Distill
**Independent audit disposition:** Ready to Close and Distill

**Recommended candidate architecture:** Five independent Values proceed to Distill. This does not accept their names, wording, complete document, or canonical installation.

| Working candidate | Plain-language name for Distill | Independent refusal point |
|---|---|---|
| Candidate 01 | Honest About What Matters | Do not preserve a materially misleading representation for convenience, appearance, speed, or profit. |
| Candidate 02 | The Client Before the Deal | Do not corrupt truthful, competent advice for transactional benefit. |
| Candidate 03 | Accountability Must Remain Clear | Do not allow material accountability to disappear or become unfairly detached from authority and reasonable ability to act. |
| Candidate 04 | Grow Without Becoming Fragile | Do not build growth on predictable, disproportionate structural degradation. |
| Candidate 05 | Build for Those Who Follow | Do not impose reasonably avoidable future loss merely because responsible continuity offers no present payoff. |

**Why five survive:** Each candidate protects a distinct relationship and changes a decision the others do not: representation of reality, loyalty in client advice, accountable ownership, responsible growth, and obligation to foreseeable future Spark people. The Candidate 04/05 merger would create an endurance umbrella, make future beneficiaries instrumental to capability, and fail the real-decision test involving preservation with no current operating benefit.

**Closure boundary:** Values Exploration 001 is sufficiently tested, mapped, and compared to serve as source material. Closing it does not make the five candidates accepted, complete, or canonical. Distill may still expose naming failure, excessive length, repeated mechanics, or a contradiction that requires one bounded reopening.

**Distill constraints:**

- Use the repository's seven-part Value design standard, but make each card materially shorter than its audit source.
- Treat the five conversational names as working names. Each must remain accurate without relying on a long corrective explanation.
- Use a short shared boundary only for genuinely common external constraints; do not turn the preamble into another Value or a disclaimer-heavy opening.
- Keep Derived Principles few, clearly phrased as candidate decision rules, and free of detailed Governance or operational controls.
- Remove repeated examples and mechanisms across cards; one primary Value should own each commitment.
- Preserve Spark Mortgages scope and do not generalize the Values to organizations broadly.

### Candidate Values Document Audit 01

**Browser disposition:** Ready for independent audit
**Independent audit disposition:** Revise before approval

**Architecture decision:** Target one canonical `01-philosophy/values.md` containing the purpose, shared boundaries, and all five Values. Do not create five additional canonical Value files or a separate canonical outline. The individual audit cards and long-form reasoning remain Workbench source material. If a shorter teaching or quick-reference artifact later proves useful, it should be derived from the accepted Values and owned by Academy or another justified downstream location—not become a competing Philosophy authority.

**What the candidate proves:**

- All five Values can coexist coherently in one document using the repository's seven-part design standard.
- The plain-language names mostly survive contact with the complete cards.
- The shared boundary successfully avoids repeating legal and professional constraints in every commitment.
- The candidate remains Spark Mortgages-specific and preserves meaningful costs and refusal points.

**Required revision:** At approximately 5,124 words and 1,123 lines, the candidate is too long for the intended single foundational document. Its self-review understates this issue. The draft repeats audit qualifications, downstream-allocation disclaimers, lists, and operational boundaries that were useful during exploration but no longer all belong in the distilled artifact.

**Compression direction:**

- Keep one short opening and five seven-part cards.
- Give each card one Commitment, a compact Meaning, three or four representative Observable Behaviors, one concise Trade-off passage, one concise Failure Mode, and only the strongest two or three proposed Derived Principles.
- Remove repeated sentences explaining that behaviors are not procedures and that downstream details belong elsewhere; establish that once in the opening or closing note.
- Remove the document-level self-review and downstream-implications report from the candidate document. They are audit output, not Values content.
- Preserve difficult boundaries, but prefer compact prose over exhaustive lists and examples.
- Reconsider `Honest About What Matters`; it remains the only name whose plain reading can imply selective honesty. `Represent Reality Honestly` remains the clearer fallback.

**Why not split:** Separate Value files would make the set harder to read as one coherent commitment structure, encourage duplicated preambles and boundaries, complicate boot and navigation, and let individual cards expand independently into mini-frameworks. Markdown headings already allow precise linking and retrieval within one file. Revisit splitting only if repeated real use demonstrates a retrieval, ownership, review-cadence, or size problem that cannot be solved through disciplined editing and heading links.

### Candidate Values Document Audit 02

**Browser disposition:** Ready for independent audit
**Independent audit disposition:** Acceptance-ready candidate

**Result:** The revision successfully compresses the candidate from approximately 5,124 words to approximately 1,880 words by an independent plain-text count while retaining one shared opening, five distinct seven-part cards, clear refusal points, meaningful trade-offs, representative behaviors, central failure modes, and three proposed downstream rules per Value.

**Confirmed improvements:**

- `Represent Reality Honestly` survives plain reading better than `Honest About What Matters` while the body preserves materiality and epistemic humility.
- Repeated allocation disclaimers, implementation detail, document-level review, and downstream reports are removed from the candidate itself.
- The five Values remain distinct after compression, including the Candidate 04/05 boundary.
- The shared opening is concise, affirmative, unranked, and appropriately bounded by external obligations.
- One-file architecture remains usable; headings support direct linking and selective retrieval.

**Minor notes that do not require another revision cycle:**

- The browser reports exactly 1,855 words using a Markdown-excluding method; an independent plain-text count to the word-count marker yields approximately 1,879. Record the method if an exact count later matters, but the difference has no substantive consequence.
- `Client interest`, `professional boundaries`, and related mortgage-sector terminology should still be checked against current applicable requirements before canonical installation or detailed downstream use.
- Proposed Derived Principles remain source signals. They should not be presented as accepted Principles or converted automatically one-for-one during the next Philosophy phase.

**Approval boundary:** The document is ready for Sheldon's substantive approval as the Values candidate for coordinated installation. Approval would not itself install or activate it. Preserve the approved text and hash, then implement through the agreed installation workflow and verify the actual repository diff independently.

**Approval recorded:** Sheldon Phillips explicitly approved the revised Spark OS Values candidate for coordinated installation on 2026-07-21. The approved local source is `spark-os-values-approved-candidate.md`, SHA-256 `49EBD75A3EF616EC04D3760376B0A3D3B1FF9BA9C6D193EB59B5F7F0C75EB17A`. It remains non-authoritative until correctly installed and independently verified.

### Installation and Audit Workflow

This task remains the continuity, audit, approval-record, and installation-instruction authority for the foundational cycle. The preferred workflow is:

1. **Audit here:** complete substantive review and resolve required changes.
2. **Approve here:** Sheldon explicitly approves the exact candidate; record its stable text and hash.
3. **Prepare here:** generate a bounded installation packet covering destinations, status changes, navigation, dependencies, glossary mappings, Governance method, and validation.
4. **Implement separately:** use another implementation task for coordinated repository edits without substantive redrafting.
5. **Verify here:** inspect the actual diff, hashes, authority state, links, and repository validation before marking installation complete.

Implementation may occur in this task if Sheldon explicitly prefers it, but that reduces reviewer/implementer separation. For foundational installation, a separate implementation task followed by verification here provides stronger independence without creating another repository authority.

| Q-019 | Open | Can organizations possess knowledge, or only preserve conditions and artifacts from which people develop it? | A model covering people, teams, repository sources, institutional routines, and AI without anthropomorphic shortcuts. | Preserve this as a central question; do not resolve it by definition alone. |
| Q-020 | Concern | Will mapping every idea into multiple documents create duplication? | A later ownership pass naming one canonical definition and using links or derived consequences elsewhere. | Allow multiple signals during exploration; require one primary owner before installation. |
| Q-021 | Resolved for current phase | Is “conceptual knowledge” distinct from Understanding in Spark OS? | The stress test did not establish a necessary unique role, and the browser response declined adoption. | Do not install “conceptual knowledge” as a Spark OS category unless later evidence reopens the question. |
| Q-022 | Resolved for current phase | Is the repository itself a knowledge base, while Spark OS is the broader Knowledge Operating System? | The response now distinguishes the authoritative repository substrate from Spark OS as the interacting whole. | Retain this component distinction, subject to final scope wording. |
| Q-023 | Resolved for current phase | Can Knowledge, Understanding, Judgment, and Competence be explored independently without fragmenting their relationships? | Five edge cases preserved distinct roles while showing iterative interaction rather than a fixed sequence. | Carry them forward as connected provisional distinctions, not separate canonical files. |
| Q-024 | Concern | Is Knowledge meaningfully defined as “what is known or justifiably represented”? | A non-circular account distinguishing truth, justification, confidence, representation, error, and the knower. | Treat the four-question table as a useful boundary sketch, not a finished ontology. |
| Q-025 | Concern | Are Repository, Brain, Governance, Academy, People, Processes, and Technology layers in a serial hierarchy? | A relationship model consistent with current repository authority and component ownership. | Reject the vertical stack as an architecture claim; describe interacting components and directed dependencies instead. |
| Q-026 | Concern | Is the Philosophy project becoming primarily an ontology project? | Continued substantive work on purpose, commitments, decision rules, and reasoning models alongside terminology. | Keep ontology subordinate to and iterative with all five documents. |
| Q-027 | Monitoring | Are Builder, Auditor, and Distiller helpful working modes without becoming permanent roles or approval gates? | Repeated use that improves outcomes while fitting the existing Workbench and acceptance lifecycle. | Use informally if helpful; do not install as governance now. |
| Q-028 | Concern | Is “Four-Dimension Capability Model” an accurate name when Knowledge and Understanding are not necessarily capabilities? | A name that describes related dimensions without imposing a shared category. | Prefer “Four-Concept Model” or “Knowledge-to-Practice Dimensions” provisionally. |
| Q-029 | Concern | Should canonical, validated, provisional, disputed, superseded, historical, and unverified become one repository status model? | A two- or multi-axis design separating authority/lifecycle, evidence confidence, currency, and historical state without duplicating existing architecture. | Park this as Governance work; do not install from Philosophy exploration. |
| Q-030 | Open | Does Spark competence require merely reliable performance, or performance appropriate to professional, ethical, and contextual standards? | Examples distinguishing technical proficiency, harmful effectiveness, professional judgment, and acceptable outcomes. | Avoid defining professional Competence as reliability alone. |
| Q-031 | Concern | What does “correct AI output” mean when causal reasoning is opaque and validation is bounded? | Explicit task, test population, confidence, failure modes, generalization limits, and independent evidence. | Say demonstrated bounded reliability rather than unqualified correctness. |
| Q-032 | Open | What responsibility does Spark OS affirm, and what outcomes does it explicitly refuse to claim or guarantee? | Manifesto language that separates purpose, support, accountability, limits, and human agency without becoming a disclaimer list. | Use the humility boundaries to constrain ambition, not dominate the Manifesto. |
| Q-033 | Concern | Does Spark OS preserve experience, or only representations and lessons derived from experience? | Language distinguishing lived practice from cases, reflections, rationale, examples, and institutional memory. | Say Spark OS preserves lessons and representations of experience; it cannot store or replace lived experience itself. |
| Q-034 | Concern | Does Spark OS distribute authority, structure it, or merely record accepted authority boundaries? | Governance-aligned language explaining allocation, delegation, accountability, and decision rights. | Prefer “structures and makes authority explicit” until distribution is deliberately established. |
| Q-035 | Monitoring | Does the five-stage Explore → Challenge → Audit → Close → Distill method improve work consistently enough to deserve later governance? | Successful repetition across several concepts or documents without creating unnecessary ceremony or a competing acceptance layer. | Use it as a Workbench convention; do not install it as Governance yet. |
| Q-036 | Concern | When is an exploration “fit for purpose” and ready to close? | Evidence that its central question has been tested, material contradictions are recorded, destination mappings exist, and further work has diminishing value for the current milestone. | Apply a pragmatic close test while preserving the right to reopen from later evidence. |
| Q-037 | Decision needed | How should Explore → Challenge → Audit → Close → Distill become a durable operating method that Full Architectural Boot reliably loads? | An installation design using an existing canonical Governance source loaded by Full Architectural Boot, without duplicating the method into Brain. | Prefer adding it to `CONTRIBUTING.md` or a justified Governance source already loaded by the Full Architectural Boot; update boot files only if necessary. |
| Q-038 | Resolved | Should the working cycle appear in full inside the Manifesto? | The revision removes the named mechanics and retains only exploration, challenge, and deliberate acceptance as philosophical commitments. | Install the full method in Governance; keep the Manifesto treatment concise. |
| Q-039 | Resolved for Manifesto | Does human responsibility account for organizational and system responsibility as well as individual accountability? | The revision explicitly assigns Spark Mortgages accountability for systems, controls, incentives, staffing, training, authority, and working conditions. | Retain reciprocal individual and organizational accountability. |
| Q-040 | Resolved | Does the Manifesto wrongly imply that instructions, checklists, or controls are inferior to understanding? | The final section explicitly calls them essential safeguards, recognizes differing depth requirements, and preserves procedural reliance for consistency, compliance, and risk control. | Retain “Capability Without Brittle Dependence.” |
| Q-041 | Resolved | Has Sheldon personally accepted the final candidate Manifesto? | Sheldon explicitly states: “I, Sheldon Phillips, approve the final Spark OS Manifesto candidate for coordinated installation.” | Record as Approved for installation; not yet Accepted/Active. |
| Q-042 | Concern | Is “Accepted Canonical Candidate” a valid status? | A status model that separates drafting, human approval, repository installation, and active authority. | Avoid the hybrid term; use Candidate → Approved for installation → Accepted/Active once installed. |
| Q-043 | Concern | Is Philosophy the “constitutional layer” from which everything else derives? | Language consistent with the existing authority map, legal and factual constraints, Governance, ADRs, Canon, and the Spark Brain Constitution. | Say Philosophy guides and constrains interpretation and design; it does not supersede or generate every other authority. |
| Q-044 | Concern | Is the first commitment client long-term outcome, client interest, truthful advice, or trusted relationship? | Cases where client preferences, uncertain outcomes, advisor judgment, revenue, lender options, and informed consent diverge. | Prefer truthful, competent advice serving the client's informed interests over a paternalistic outcome claim. |
| Q-045 | Concern | Is “long-term capability over short-term advantage” a genuine Value or an overbroad summary of several Values? | Counterexamples where immediate action, client urgency, legal duty, or short-term protection should override longer-horizon optimization. | Keep as a candidate pattern; do not let it absorb truthfulness, client interest, accountability, learning, and competence. |
| Q-046 | Resolved for current phase | Can “problems per $1M funded” measure healthy growth without distorting reporting or case selection? | Sheldon clarifies it was figurative speech expressing preference for fewer avoidable problems at lower volume, not a KPI or comparison metric. | Preserve the $200M/$400M contrast as a possible Academy example; do not install the ratio as a metric. |
| Q-047 | Open | What deserves to become institutional memory? | Selection criteria covering future judgment, repeated learning cost, accountability, legal retention, risk, privacy, sensitivity, retrieval value, and maintenance burden. | “Future judgment would be poorer without it” is strong but incomplete. |
| Q-048 | Concern | Who remains accountable when automation participates in a process? | A responsibility model distinguishing process owner, system maintainer, decision owner, approver, operator, vendor, and organizational accountability. | Do not assign all responsibility solely to the person maintaining the automated process. |
| Q-049 | Concern | Does “truthful advice regardless of context” mean the Value is unconditional or that advice itself ignores context? | Cases where facts are uncertain, disclosure is constrained, client goals differ, and timing affects what can responsibly be said. | Keep truthfulness non-negotiable while making advice contextual, evidence-aware, and honest about uncertainty. |
| Q-050 | Concern | Is immediate client protection intrinsically required or justified because every client may become future business? | A case where the client will never return, refer, or benefit Spark financially. | Protect the client's informed interests regardless of future organizational benefit. |
| Q-051 | Concern | When may speed responsibly trade against precision? | Cases differentiated by stakes, reversibility, detectability, correction cost, client consent, deadlines, and available safeguards. | Do not treat preventable client-facing errors as desirable relationship touchpoints. |
| Q-052 | Concern | When may professional judgment deviate from process? | A rule covering purpose, evidence, authority, legal/regulatory/contractual limits, risk, documentation, escalation, reversibility, and later review. | Explainability alone is insufficient; deviation must be authorized, accountable, and bounded. |
| Q-053 | Decision needed | What counts as a “decision” that must remain human? | A taxonomy separating consequential judgment and client recommendations from calculations, classifications, routing, reminders, rule execution, and administrative determinations. | Preserve named human accountability for consequential outcomes; test whether every bounded machine determination requires individual human selection. |
| Q-054 | Concern | Can institutional knowledge be “compiled without loss of content”? | Examples comparing summaries, canonical synthesis, source preservation, archive, deletion, and retrieval needs. | Treat synthesis as purposeful loss of detail; preserve source material only when its enduring value justifies the cost and risk. |
| Q-055 | Concern | Is capability the single outcome toward which all Values point? | Counterexamples involving client interest, integrity, legal duty, fairness, privacy, and ethical restraint even when they reduce capability or efficiency. | Treat capability as central but not morally sufficient or universally dominant. |
| Q-056 | Open | Is epistemic honesty part of truthful client advice or a distinct Value? | Cases involving uncertainty, forecasts, incomplete evidence, disagreement, correction, and organizational reporting beyond client advice. | It may be broader than client advice because it also governs metrics, institutional memory, AI, and internal learning. |
| Q-057 | Concern | Must canonical guidance always link every supporting source? | A proportional traceability rule based on consequence, volatility, dispute, legal significance, and need to reconstruct reasoning. | Require sufficient provenance and rationale, not universal exhaustive linking. |
| Q-058 | Concern | When does an automated output become an adverse consequential determination requiring human review? | A taxonomy covering exclusion, client impact, materiality, reversibility, false-negative harm, appeal, and aggregation effects. | Automation may triage or recommend; it should not silently eliminate viable clients without accountable review and recourse. |
| Q-059 | Concern | Is “faithfulness to reality” a coherent Value or an abstract umbrella that merges several commitments? | Tests involving confidentiality, privacy, legally restricted disclosure, simplifying for comprehension, forecasts, disputed facts, uncertainty, and incomplete evidence. | Prefer concrete commitment language over philosophical jargon unless the umbrella survives these limits. |
| Q-060 | Concern | Does every boundary scenario necessarily produce a unique Value, Principle, Governance owner, and repository owner? | Cases where a scenario only strengthens an existing Value, has no new Principle, or is governed by an external obligation. | Use the fields as prompts, not mandatory new artifacts or a linear derivation pipeline. |
| Q-061 | Concern | Is “responsible reasoning over mechanical compliance” a Value or already a Principle? | A non-negotiable costly commitment distinct from the decision rule permitting process deviation. | Candidate Value may be refusal to surrender responsibility to procedure; exact wording remains unresolved. |
| Q-062 | Concern | Is “preserving meaning over minimizing information” a false trade-off? | Cases where careful simplification improves meaning, accessibility, and use while retaining proportional traceability. | Favor faithful, usable institutional memory rather than information volume or maximal retention. |
| Q-063 | Concern | Should the Values document mainly expand by adding new Values? | Evidence that a newly observed commitment is not already protected by an existing Value and cannot be handled through wording, Principles, or examples. | Make the document extensible but require consolidation, merge, refinement, or retirement when the set develops overlap or contradiction. |
| Q-064 | Decision needed | Are truthful client advice and faithfulness to reality one Value or two? | Cases where Spark accurately discloses reality but still prioritizes its transaction over the client's informed interest. | Treat them as provisionally distinct because truthfulness does not by itself establish client loyalty or care. |
| Q-065 | Resolved for candidate audit | Is Candidate 03 fundamentally about responsibility, accountability, ownership, or the relationship among them? | A seven-part card that permits legitimate delegation while preventing ownership gaps and unfair assignment of accountability without authority. | The substantive commitment is explicit, proportionate accountable ownership; the final memorable name remains for set comparison. |
| Q-066 | Monitoring | When can accountability be shared without being diffused? | Boundary cases for committees, handoffs, joint professional work, vendors, and organization-level system ownership with named decision and escalation rights. | Shared accountability is valid when material duties, decision rights, monitoring, follow-up, and escalation remain locatable; no single person must own every consequence. |
| Q-067 | Decision needed | Is Capability Before Scale a morally distinct commitment or merely an outcome produced by the first three Values? | Cases where growth remains truthful, client-aligned, and accountable but predictably degrades competence, resilience, workload, or service quality. | Candidate 04 must demonstrate what Spark will refuse and pay to protect without elevating capability above ethics or external obligations. |
| Q-068 | Concern | Is sustainable capability a Value, an outcome, or a principle limiting growth? | A refusal point showing what Spark will actually delay or lose to prevent scale from degrading quality, resilience, or people. | Keep as a Value candidate because Sheldon accepts slower growth, but audit its wording carefully. |
| Q-069 | Concern | Is knowledge stewardship independently non-negotiable or an implementation of capability and future responsibility? | Cases where preservation is costly, provides no near-term operational benefit, but materially serves future Spark people or accountability. | Retain as a candidate and test its distinct intergenerational commitment. |
| Q-070 | Concern | Is “Faithfulness to Material Reality” memorable and natural enough to function as a Value name? | Plain-language alternatives tested against the full commitment and failure cases. | Retain the concept but refine the name during set comparison. |
| Q-071 | Concern | Does a “knowing distortion” standard permit willful blindness or careless failure to verify? | Language covering credible contrary evidence, reasonable verification, and correction duties without demanding perfect knowledge. | Expand the standard beyond deliberate deception while preserving honest-error protection. |
| Q-072 | Concern | Is `Client Interest Before Transaction` the right name? | Alternatives tested for memorability, informed autonomy, professional boundaries, and non-paternalistic meaning. | Keep the commitment; refine the title during set comparison if necessary. |
| Q-073 | Concern | Does “the same quality of advice regardless of profitability” require identical service effort for every client? | Language separating equal professional integrity from disclosed service scope, feasibility, capacity, and reasonable boundaries. | Require consistent honesty, competence, and care—not unlimited or identical service. |
| Q-074 | Concern | How far must Spark search for and disclose external alternatives? | A reasonable standard based on known material alternatives, competence, service scope, evidence, and client circumstances. | Do not imply exhaustive market search or knowledge of every external option. |
| Q-075 | Decision needed | Is Stewardship Across Time independently non-negotiable, or is it an implementation of responsible capability and accountability? | Cases where preservation has meaningful present cost and no near-term operating benefit but prevents avoidable future relearning, lost rationale, repeated harm, or unfair burden on future Spark people. | Candidate 05 must protect selective, responsible continuity without becoming “preserve everything.” |
| Q-076 | Concern | Do Candidates 04 and 05 express one broad commitment to durable organizational capability? | Set comparison showing whether refusing structurally degrading growth and accepting present cost for future continuity produce distinct decisions and failure modes. | Keep separate through Candidate 05 Audit, then explicitly test merger. |
| Q-077 | Decision needed | What is the smallest credible initial Values set after all five candidates received Refine? | Pairwise overlap tests, distinct refusal points, coverage gaps, memorable naming, conflict analysis, and removal of downstream mechanics. | Begin complete-set comparison without assuming five final Values. |
| Q-078 | Decision needed | Does Candidate 05's obligation to absent future beneficiaries survive without becoming a broad duty to undefined future people? | Cases where present preservation cost serves future Spark employees, leaders, maintainers, or clients affected by continuity but offers no current capability benefit. | Bound the beneficiary and test whether the moral refusal remains distinct from Candidates 03–04. |
| Q-079 | Resolved for Distill | What is the smallest credible initial Values set supported by the current evidence? | Complete-set comparison preserving distinct beneficiaries, costs, refusal points, and real-decision differences. | Five independent candidates proceed to Distill; this remains a candidate architecture, not acceptance. |
| Q-080 | Monitoring | Do the five plain-language names remain accurate and durable when read without their audit histories? | A concise complete Values document in which each name, commitment, Meaning, Trade-off, and Failure Mode reinforce rather than correct one another. | Test during Distill; reopen only the specific name or candidate if compression creates material ambiguity. |
| Q-081 | Decision needed | Should the candidate Values document include one shared external-constraints preamble? | A concise statement that prevents repetitive legal and professional caveats without creating a hierarchy or implying every conflict is resolved. | Permit a short shared boundary during Distill and audit whether it clarifies or dilutes the Values. |
| Q-082 | Resolved for current architecture | Should each Value become its own canonical document beneath a Values outline? | Evidence that independent files materially improve retrieval, ownership, review cadence, or boot performance without duplicating authority or weakening set coherence. | No. Use one canonical `values.md`; retain detailed audits in Workbench and create derivative summaries downstream only when a demonstrated audience need exists. |
| Q-083 | Decision needed | How much compression can the candidate Values document sustain without losing the distinctions earned during Audit? | A revised one-file candidate that preserves all five refusal points, costs, key boundaries, and seven-part cards while removing audit scaffolding. | The 5,124-word candidate requires a focused tightening pass before approval review. |
| Q-084 | Resolved | Did the focused compression preserve the five-Value architecture and seven-part standard? | A complete concise candidate retaining distinct commitments, costs, behaviors, failure modes, and minimal downstream signals. | Yes. The approximately 1,880-word candidate is acceptance-ready. |
| Q-085 | Decision needed | Should coordinated foundational installation be implemented in this audit task or a separate implementation task? | A workflow balancing continuity, bounded authority, efficiency, and independent verification. | Prefer generating the installation packet here, implementing it separately, and auditing the resulting diff here; implement here only by Sheldon's explicit preference. |
| Q-086 | Decision needed | What current mortgage-insurance thresholds, category rules, and authoritative external sources should Spark accept for the prototype topic? | Licensed SME review plus dated authoritative sources capable of supporting Canon. | Resolve during Canon development before the prototype can leave Workbench; do not fill placeholders from AI recollection. |
| Q-087 | Decision needed | What canonical term should Spark use for the middle mortgage-insurance category? | Confirmed underlying concept, current industry and regulatory usage, and a term that remains clear to learners and practitioners. | Defer to Canon and later Glossary reconciliation; the prototype label remains provisional. |
| Q-088 | Decision needed | When should a new agent verify independently versus escalate uncertainty to a supervisor? | A role- and consequence-sensitive operating rule aligned with delegated authority, competence, client impact, and available support. | Preserve as a Governance, Canon, and Academy design question; do not let the prototype invent the threshold. |
| Q-089 | Monitoring | Does Course Prototype 001 merit revision, a second vertical slice, preservation only, or eventual Academy distillation after cross-platform review? | Reconciled independent reviews, SME input, source availability, and evidence about learning or operational usefulness. | Keep PR #12 draft and unmerged through cross-platform review. |
| Q-090 | Resolved | What distinct findings did the other independent reviewer produce for Course Prototype 001? | A second report whose content or hash differs from the report initially supplied twice. | Received distinct 124-line report with SHA-256 `4413EA152F897F5AB9C8EB4ABA38158CB4706B804B27F2CF9C57C3C42D32D750`; reconcile under S-086. |

## Live Provisional Glossary

This glossary captures language emerging from the conversation. It is exploratory and has no authority over `01-philosophy/glossary.md` or any other repository source. A term becomes canonical only after its definition, distinctions, ownership, and consequences are reviewed and explicitly accepted.

| Term | Provisional meaning | Important distinction or unresolved issue | Status | Likely canonical owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Spark | The mortgage brokerage, its people, culture, clients, professional standards, and organizational commitments. | Must not be used interchangeably with Spark OS. | Working distinction | Glossary or organizational Canon; destination unresolved |
| Spark OS | The knowledge operating system for Spark Mortgages, connecting authoritative knowledge, governance, navigation, learning, judgment support, and capability development. | Broader multi-organization scope has been proposed but not accepted. | Repository-aligned working definition | Manifesto and Glossary |
| Repository | The durable Markdown-based substrate in which Spark OS preserves authoritative and exploratory artifacts according to assigned locations. | The repository is a component of Spark OS, not the whole operating system. | Working definition | Glossary / Governance |
| Information | Encoded content, signals, facts, claims, or instructions available for interpretation. | Whether information exists independently of an interpreter remains open. Information may be false or unverified. | Provisional | Glossary |
| Knowledge | Provisionally, warranted acceptance of something as true, reliable, or effective within a defined context. | Acceptance may be warranted yet false; “effective” needs scope and ethical limits; the knower and evidence standard remain unresolved. Distinct from its repository representation. | Tested but provisional | Glossary, with implications elsewhere |
| Knowledge representation | An artifact that preserves facts, procedures, models, decisions, rationale, definitions, or relationships in a form that can be reviewed and used. | A representation does not guarantee truth, understanding, or competent application. | Strong working definition | Glossary / Governance |
| Factual knowledge | Knowledge that something is the case. | Requires a truth, evidence, confidence, and change-over-time model before canonical use. | Provisional external taxonomy | Glossary / Canon standards |
| Procedural knowledge | Knowledge concerning how an action or process is performed. | A written procedure is a representation; reliable performance may instead demonstrate competence. | Provisional external taxonomy | Glossary / Mental Models |
| Conceptual knowledge | Proposed knowledge of relationships, principles, explanations, and why things work. | May duplicate the intended meaning of Understanding. | Unresolved | Glossary or Mental Models |
| Understanding | Grasp of meaning, relationships, implications, and reasons; provisionally answers “What does it mean?” | Its boundary with conceptual knowledge and its relationship to Knowledge remain unresolved. | Active exploration | Glossary / Mental Models |
| Judgment | Evaluation or selection of what should be believed, prioritized, recommended, or done under context, trade-offs, risk, or uncertainty. | “What should be done?” is useful but too narrow for every form of judgment. Possessing knowledge does not guarantee sound judgment. | Provisional | Glossary / Principles / Mental Models |
| Capability | The capacity to perform or achieve an outcome. | May exist at individual, team, or organizational level; relationship to Competence requires refinement. | Provisional | Glossary |
| Competence | Demonstrated ability to perform or decide reliably within a defined domain and context. | Reliability alone may describe harmful or obsolete proficiency; professional Competence may also require appropriate standards, judgment, currency, and ethics. | Tested but provisional | Manifesto, Values, Principles, and Glossary |
| Trust | Confidence granted or earned on the basis of demonstrated reliability, competence, integrity, or appropriate safeguards. | Whether confidence precedes trust in every case remains provisional. | Active exploration | Values, Principles, and Glossary |
| Value | A non-negotiable commitment Spark is willing to incur meaningful cost to preserve. | Behaviors and qualities are evidence or consequences, not the Value itself. | Strong repository-aligned definition | Values and Glossary |
| Principle | A durable decision rule derived from one or more accepted Values. | Must not become a container for every axiom, observation, or truth claim. | Strong repository-aligned definition | Principles and Glossary |
| Mental Model | A reusable way of interpreting problems, dependencies, patterns, or trade-offs to improve judgment. | Supports but does not replace judgment or create authority by itself. | Repository-aligned definition | Mental Models and Glossary |
| Manifesto | The document that explains why Spark OS exists, the problem it addresses, and the future it seeks to create. | The separate organizational Spark Manifesto has no accepted repository destination yet. | Repository-aligned boundary | Manifesto and Glossary |
| Canonical | Deliberately accepted as authoritative within an assigned repository location. | Polished, repeated, or historically preserved content is not automatically canonical. | Repository-aligned definition | Governance and Glossary |
| Candidate | A proposed document or concept undergoing exploration, audit, review, or approval. | Quality, completeness, AI endorsement, and Workbench closure do not grant authority. | Strong working definition | Governance / Glossary |
| Approved for installation | Explicitly accepted by the authorized human decision owner for placement into its intended repository authority, but not yet installed and active there. | Distinct from a browser assistant's recommendation and from current canonical authority. | Proposed workflow status | Governance / Glossary |
| Accepted / Active | Installed in the assigned authoritative repository location following required review and Governance. | Remains revisable and may later be superseded; accepted does not mean infallible or finished. | Proposed clarification | Governance / Glossary |
| Constitutional layer | A metaphor for highly foundational constraints governing downstream choices. | Risky terminology in Spark OS because Governance, ADRs, legal obligations, Canon, and the Spark Brain Constitution have distinct authority; Philosophy is foundational but not supreme over every source. | Discouraged pending clarification | Governance / Glossary |
| Authority | The assigned right of a repository source to establish the accepted meaning or rule for a type of knowledge. | Conversation, Workbench, and Design History do not establish current authority. | Working definition | Governance and Glossary |
| Workbench | The non-authoritative repository area for drafts, alternatives, unresolved questions, and exploratory work. | Thought Papers are a possible Workbench form, not another lifecycle layer. | Repository-aligned definition | Governance and Glossary |
| Thought Paper | An optional exploratory Workbench draft focused on examining an idea without granting it permanent or canonical status. | Must still retain status, provenance, review boundaries, and intended consolidation direction. | Provisional drafting form | Workbench only unless methodology is later accepted |
| Knowledge distillation | The iterative process of exploring, challenging, connecting, validating, and simplifying ideas into clearer candidate knowledge. | Does not itself accept content or replace the repository's contribution workflow. | Provisional methodology | Workbench; possible future Governance |
| Continuous mapping | Recording how exploratory ideas may contribute to Manifesto, Values, Principles, Mental Models, and Glossary. | Multiple signals are allowed during exploration, but one primary owner is required before installation. | Working convention | Workbench audit |
| Order | A proposed pattern that consequential dependencies exist within some systems and capabilities. | Must not be assumed to be a universal or discoverable foundation of reality. | Candidate concept | Principles or Mental Models |
| Spark Brain | The repository control plane for startup, navigation, continuity, and integrity. | It does not own or transform Philosophy, Canon, or human capability. | Accepted repository boundary | Governance and Glossary |
| Ontology | An explicit account of the concepts, categories, distinctions, and relationships used by Spark OS. | Supports coherent reasoning but must not displace purpose, commitments, decision rules, or practical models. | Provisional | Glossary / Mental Models; possible Governance implications |
| Builder mode | An informal mode that generates, connects, and develops candidate ideas. | Not a permanent AI or human role and does not grant authority. | Provisional working mode | Workbench only |
| Auditor mode | An informal mode that challenges assumptions, boundaries, overlap, consistency, and evidence. | Critique does not accept or own the resulting knowledge. | Active working mode | Workbench only |
| Distiller mode | An informal mode that synthesizes explored and reviewed material into simpler candidate canonical content. | Distillation does not bypass Sheldon’s acceptance or repository authority rules. | Provisional working mode | Workbench only |
| Established knowledge | Relevant claims, models, or findings supported by credible prior work and appropriate evidence. | Established does not mean infallible, current, context-free, or automatically canonical for Spark OS. | Provisional | Principles / Governance / Glossary |
| Human agency | A person's active participation, choice, effort, interpretation, practice, and responsibility in learning and action. | Spark OS may support agency but cannot replace it or guarantee resulting capability. | Provisional | Manifesto / Values / Glossary |
| Misinformation | A false or misleading claim represented or communicated as information. | Confident belief, internal coherence, authority, repetition, or successful application does not turn it into Knowledge. Intent is not yet part of this working definition. | Provisional | Glossary / Governance |
| Stale fact | A representation that was accurate within an earlier time or context but is no longer safe to treat as current. | Historical truth, current applicability, authority, and supersession are separate properties. | Provisional | Glossary / Governance / Canon standards |
| Tacit expertise | Pattern recognition, practical understanding, or skilled judgment expressed reliably but only partly articulable. | Inability to explain does not prove absence of knowledge; success does not establish infallibility or currency. | Provisional | Mental Models / Glossary |
| Disputed claim | A claim for which qualified or relevant parties reach materially different conclusions from available evidence or assumptions. | Disagreement alone does not make all positions equally warranted or prevent a necessary provisional decision. | Provisional | Governance / Glossary |
| Epistemic status | The current evidentiary standing of a claim or representation, such as verified, disputed, or unverified. | Must be kept separate from repository authority, lifecycle location, temporal currency, and archive state. | Candidate Governance concept | Governance / Glossary |
| Evidence | Information, observation, source material, or other grounds relevant to supporting, weakening, or changing a claim or decision. | Evidence must still be interpreted; existence, authority, volume, or agreement does not automatically establish what conclusion it warrants. | Principles Exploration 001 term | Principles / Mental Models / Glossary |
| Interpretation | Meaning or implication drawn from evidence through reasoning, context, judgment, or a model. | Interpretation is not inferior to evidence and cannot always be cleanly separated from observation; it should not be presented as more directly established than it is. | Principles Exploration 001 term | Principles / Mental Models / Glossary |
| Assumption | A proposition treated as true or sufficiently reliable for a current analysis or decision without being fully established within that context. | Assumptions may be necessary; material assumptions should remain visible and open to proportionate challenge. | Principles Exploration 001 term | Principles / Mental Models / Glossary |
| Uncertainty | A limitation in what is known, predicted, interpreted, or controlled that may affect confidence, choice, risk, or action. | Uncertainty does not prohibit action; its materiality and consequences should shape explanation, judgment, safeguards, and review. | Principles Exploration 001 term | Principles / Mental Models / Glossary |
| Contrary evidence | Evidence that materially weakens, qualifies, or creates reason to reconsider an existing claim, conclusion, assumption, or decision. | Contrary does not mean decisive, equally credible, current, or sufficient to reverse the conclusion; attention remains proportionate to relevance, support, and consequence. | Principles Exploration 001 term | Principles / Mental Models / Glossary |
| Corrigibility | The capacity and willingness of a person, conclusion, system, or institution to remain open to justified correction when relevant evidence exposes error or limitation. | Does not require endless reopening, equal treatment of unsupported claims, or abandonment of necessary provisional decisions. | Strong Candidate 02 concept | Principles / Mental Models / Glossary |
| Provenance | Information about a representation's source, authorship, evidence, derivation, and change history. | Provenance supports evaluation but does not guarantee truth or authority. | Strong candidate | Governance / Glossary |
| Currency | The degree to which a representation remains current and applicable for its stated time, jurisdiction, assumptions, and context. | A canonical source can become stale; authority does not guarantee currency. | Strong candidate | Governance / Canon standards / Glossary |
| Semantic drift | Gradual divergence in meaning, assumptions, implications, or guidance among related authoritative and derivative materials even when their files and links remain structurally valid. | Cannot be eliminated by structural validation alone; review should be proportionate to dependency, consequence, and observed change. | Strong candidate | Governance / Glossary |
| Point-in-time applicability | The time-bounded conditions under which a representation was accepted or applicable, including effective dates, jurisdiction, assumptions, and supersession. | Git history records change but does not by itself tell a user which rule governed a past case. | Strong candidate | Canon standards / Governance / Glossary |
| Content model | The accepted structure used to represent a category of knowledge, including required fields, relationships, evidence, temporal status, and human-readable explanation. | Should emerge from real use cases and downstream needs rather than a universal schema designed before content exists. | Provisional | Canon Governance / Glossary |
| Review currency | Evidence that material has been reviewed within a period appropriate to its volatility, consequence, and external obligations. | A recent date does not prove correctness, and a universal expiry period may create ceremony or false assurance. | Provisional | Canon Governance / Glossary |
| Serialization contract | The exact syntax and parser expectations governing a machine-readable repository file. | A familiar extension must not imply unsupported syntax; the documented format and actual parser must agree. | Strong technical-governance term | Governance / Brain |
| Anchor integrity | The condition in which an internal Markdown link resolves not only to an existing file but also to the intended heading or fragment within it. | Requires an accepted slugging convention and tests; file existence alone does not protect against heading drift. | Strong validation concept | Governance / Brain |
| Branch protection | Repository-host controls that restrict direct changes to important branches and can require pull requests, checks, or other merge conditions. | Must fit the actual contributor model; requiring independent approval can deadlock a sole-owner repository. | Strong operational control | Governance / GitHub configuration |
| Epistemic opacity | A condition in which an output's performance can be evaluated while some causal or internal reasoning remains unavailable or not meaningfully understood. | Opacity may be partial; validated performance does not establish complete understanding or unrestricted generalization. | Provisional | Mental Models / AI Governance / Glossary |
| Human responsibility | Continuing human accountability for consequential interpretation, judgment, oversight, and action. | Tool assistance and automation may change how responsibility is exercised but should not erase accountable ownership. | Strong candidate | Manifesto / Values / Principles / Glossary |
| Close | A deliberate Workbench decision to stop expanding an exploration once it is sufficiently tested and mapped for the current purpose. | Closure does not mean truth, acceptance, completeness, permanence, or canonical status; later evidence may reopen the work. | Active working convention | Workbench only; possible future Governance |
| Fit for purpose | Sufficiently clear, tested, bounded, and useful for the current intended task or decision. | Does not mean perfect, universally true, final, or suitable for every future use. | Provisional | Principles / Mental Models / Glossary |
| Responsibility | An obligation to exercise appropriate care, judgment, action, and accountability for consequences within an assigned role or decision. | Must be distinguished from authority, blame, capability, and guaranteed control over outcomes. | Provisional | Values / Principles / Glossary |
| Experience | Lived exposure, practice, feedback, and accumulated encounters that may shape recognition, understanding, judgment, and competence. | Repositories can preserve reflections and representations of experience but cannot reproduce lived experience itself. | Provisional | Manifesto / Mental Models / Glossary |
| Trustworthy organization | An organization whose systems, people, decisions, and conduct consistently provide justified grounds for reliance. | Remains broader than the active Spark Mortgages scope and must not imply perfection or guaranteed outcomes. | Provisional | Manifesto / Values / Glossary |
| Institutional memory | Durable representations of decisions, rationale, lessons, cases, history, and context that allow an organization to learn across time and personnel changes. | Memory can be incomplete, stale, disputed, or superseded and must not be equated with current truth. | Strong candidate | Manifesto / Mental Models / Glossary |
| Oversight | Deliberate review, monitoring, supervision, or control proportionate to authority, risk, opacity, and consequences. | Does not necessarily require a human to review every low-risk action; ownership and escalation must remain clear. | Provisional | Principles / Governance / Glossary |
| Accountability | Answerability for decisions, conduct, assigned responsibilities, and consequences, including duties to explain, correct, or improve. | Must include organizational and system ownership rather than locating every failure in an individual. | Provisional | Values / Principles / Governance / Glossary |
| Accountable owner | The person, role, group, or organization explicitly answerable for a defined responsibility, decision, system, or consequence. | A named owner must have proportionate authority and cannot erase contributing or organizational accountability. | Provisional Candidate 03 concept | Values / Principles / Governance / Glossary |
| Responsibility gap | A condition in which a material duty, process, decision, system, or consequence has no clear accountable owner, often because work is automated, delegated, shared, or assumed. | Can exist during apparent success; failure is not required before the gap matters. | Strong Candidate 03 failure mode | Values / Governance / Glossary |
| Proportionate accountability | Accountability aligned with a person's or organization's role, authority, knowledge, influence, and reasonable ability to prevent, detect, escalate, or correct an outcome. | Does not excuse neglect within one's role or eliminate Spark Mortgages' responsibility for systemic conditions. | Provisional | Values / Principles / Governance / Glossary |
| Delegation | Deliberate assignment of work, bounded authority, or defined responsibility to another person, group, system, or external party. | Legitimate delegation requires scope, limits, competence, escalation, and retained duties; it does not make accountability disappear. | Strong Candidate 03 distinction | Principles / Governance / Glossary |
