### S-017 — 2026-07-21 — Sheldon Phillips and browser conversation

**Sheldon's Values trade-offs:**

1. Truthful advice should prevail over short-term revenue, including recommending delay or a bank when that is genuinely the reasonable client solution.
2. Growth should be evaluated through time and problem burden rather than funding volume or agent count alone; a seamless $200M brokerage is preferable to a chaotic $400M brokerage.
3. Slower implementation is justified only when the clarity deficit cannot responsibly be overcome after proceeding; bounded action may be necessary to acquire clarity and avoid analysis paralysis.
4. Institutional memory should be preserved when its loss would destroy meaningful value or force avoidable relearning, while obsolete knowledge may sometimes inhibit better solutions.
5. Automation may improve ease and consistency but must not replace accountability; automated mistakes require accountable maintenance and response.

**Pasted exchange SHA-256:** `E1F8BB41F6BFB22DF446995248F63D70BFCD6DEF23F6581325A237C018D3BBA9`

**Browser response sequence:**

1. It identifies the answers as trade-off decisions rather than generic corporate Values.
2. It reframes truthful advice as client long-term outcome over immediate transaction.
3. It reframes competence versus growth as healthy organizational capability over growth for its own sake and praises “problems per $1M funded.”
4. It identifies sufficient clarity for responsible action rather than clarity-first or action-first extremes.
5. It identifies selective institutional memory and warns against an organizational museum.
6. It reframes automation as work performance without transferred accountability.
7. It proposes “short-term advantage sacrificed for long-term capability” as a possible unifying Value.
8. It challenges cost-based memory selection and proposes future judgment as the deeper preservation criterion.
9. It advances the exploration into uncomfortable refusal, delegation, consistency, memory, speed, and future-employee questions.

**Audit note:** This is a productive Explore result and should now enter Challenge. The response correctly finds trade-offs, but its elegant umbrella risks erasing distinct commitments. Client interest must not become paternalistic outcome selection or be justified by expected repeat business. The proposed operational metric needs separate validation. Institutional-memory selection must consider more than future judgment, and automation accountability must be distributed across actual roles rather than assigned only to a maintainer.

### S-018 — 2026-07-21 — Sheldon Phillips and browser conversation

**Sheldon's Challenge answers:**

1. Truthful advice is unconditional and unaffected by whether a client returns.
2. Immediate client protection is treated as inseparable from long-term Spark capability, partly because current clients may become future clients or referral sources.
3. Speed versus precision is situational and requires competence and judgment; corrections may sometimes create additional relationship contact.
4. Explainable professional judgment is said to supersede process.
5. All decisions are proposed to remain human, with automation limited to expedited, consistent process execution and increased human bandwidth.
6. Obsolete institutional material should be reduced, compiled, archived, or removed as appropriate, while external legal and regulatory retention rules always prevail.
7. Future employees should inherit today's hard-earned knowledge and face a lower barrier to mortgage competence.
8. Healthy capability may appear as improved margins and bandwidth without corresponding growth in agent count or funded volume.

**Sheldon-response SHA-256:** `970764477D42D0EB999516C434956E5BCA502AAAF449005E1F9CB801C5632DC3`
**Browser-response SHA-256:** `05B7F8177957657AD834DD505BC0DBED88ABA334CDB02E78E9C39A3984DE7719`

**Browser response sequence:**

1. It advances truthfulness as unconditional and independent of business benefit.
2. It treats client protection and long-term capability as manifestations of one commitment.
3. It identifies competence as an integrating concept for speed and precision.
4. It proposes explainable, accountable professional judgment over process, then correctly challenges the word “always” using legal and regulatory constraints.
5. It frames automation as removing cognitive friction rather than decision-making.
6. It proposes knowledge compression and a new linear institutional-memory lifecycle.
7. It identifies stewardship toward future employees and capability-oriented operational evidence.
8. It again proposes capability as the recurring philosophy behind most Spark systems.
9. It concludes that Spark seeks capable, accountable professionals exercising trustworthy judgment in service of clients.

**Audit note:** The Challenge strengthens truthfulness, client interest, stewardship, and accountable professional judgment as distinct signals. It also exposes several unsafe absolutes. The stable Value may be unconditional, but advice remains contextual. Client protection must not depend on future business. Explainability does not authorize process deviation. “All decisions human” requires a definition of consequential decision. Lossless knowledge compression is unrealistic, and the proposed lifecycle must not compete with existing repository Governance. Capability is central but cannot absorb truthfulness, legal duty, client interest, or ethical restraint.

### S-019 — 2026-07-21 — Sheldon Phillips, browser conversation, and Codex boundary tests

**Sheldon's clarifications:**

- Legal and regulatory rules are intentional no-exception boundaries; professional-judgment flexibility concerns only discretionary Spark-specific processes within Spark's organizational umbrella.
- “Problems per $1M” was figurative language for preferring fewer avoidable problems at lower volume over more revenue with more chaos, not a proposed KPI.
- Sheldon requests concrete boundary testing around uncertainty, process deviation, consequential automation, summarization loss, metric gaming, and obligations overriding capability.

**Browser-response SHA-256:** `BBCEE502A68987581B6E7EA8BD06A57C69A208E4F3167C731D333236472379AF`

**Browser response sequence:**

1. It accepts the illustrative rather than metric role of the production example.
2. It recognizes capability as morally neutral rather than the supreme Value.
3. It supports bounded professional judgment and a more implementable automation taxonomy.
4. It accepts that summarization loses information and rejects another knowledge lifecycle.
5. It proposes six final boundary scenarios covering uncertainty, deviation, automation, summarization, metrics, and external obligation.

**Codex boundary-test results:**

1. **Uncertainty:** The best advice distinguishes evidence, uncertainty, forecasts, scenario consequences, and client priorities. “I don't know” can be truthful but is professionally incomplete without useful interpretation. Trust may be supported by honest uncertainty but is not guaranteed.
2. **Authorized deviation:** Reversing a low-risk internal sequence can be legitimate when within authority and all external or non-discretionary constraints remain satisfied. Documentation and review should be proportionate; recurring exceptions should trigger process review.
3. **Consequential automation:** Silent removal of potentially viable applicants is a material adverse determination. Aggregate accuracy cannot justify hidden exclusion; accountable review, exception handling, monitoring, and recourse are required.
4. **Summarization loss:** Condensation can remove provenance, rationale, dissent, temporal context, and edge cases. Canonical guidance needs proportional traceability, not universal preservation of every source artifact.
5. **Metric gaming:** Transparent reporting better reflects Spark's emerging character than artificially favorable numbers. Metrics that reward concealment should be redesigned, contextualized, or retired.
6. **Overriding obligations:** Applicable external obligations govern even when a broker believes another result is technically better. Disagreement belongs in authorized legal, policy, escalation, or advocacy channels—not an undisclosed exception in a client file.

**Audit note:** The Value candidates survive the tests, but several operational implications belong elsewhere. Truthful client service, epistemic honesty, accountability, knowledge stewardship, and sustainable capability are Value signals. Process deviation, automation review, proportional traceability, and metric design are candidate Principles or Governance. Values Exploration 001 is nearing Audit readiness once Sheldon and the browser conversation react to these completed test results.

### S-040 — 2026-07-22 — External Claude repository audit

**Context:** Sheldon supplied a conversation containing Claude Code's repository summary, Claude's architectural critique, Sheldon's clarification that Spark OS is a system for producing future content, and six follow-up architecture suggestions.

**Source SHA-256:** `F8585486E8E4F8BCB60DF6B13E5876C758F1E7169F8324E27C8CDA076018D7D5`

**Independent audit:**

1. Structural integrity versus semantic correctness is the strongest valid observation and is already acknowledged by Repository Health; detailed Canon review governance remains future work.
2. Point-in-time applicability, effective dates, supersession, evidence, and risk-based review currency are genuine pre-Canon requirements.
3. Canon representation should be tested against a real mortgage use case after Principles rather than settled now as an abstract YAML/JSON schema.
4. Semantic drift and cascading downstream review are valid concerns but cannot be solved through universal Principle links or semantic CI.
5. The manifest-amendment criticism is overstated because material Brain and integrity changes already use the ADR and pull-request workflow; only possible clarification remains.
6. The AI portability criticism is already resolved by active AI Governance and requires no new architecture.
7. Conflict escalation exists; the remaining need is a proportionate downstream change-impact workflow after Principles.
8. Sheldon as current decision owner is an intentional present-stage authority, while succession and delegated acceptance remain later scaling concerns rather than current blockers.

**Disposition:** Accept the valid temporal, semantic, and validation concerns as downstream tasks. Do not let them displace Principles or create a new prerequisite governance layer.

8. The validator has no test suite and should receive fixtures before substantial expansion.

10. A LICENSE requires an explicit business/legal reuse decision and must not be added as routine hygiene.

A bounded technical-integrity maintenance track now ranks alongside it and should complete before the next canonical installation, while temporal, semantic, and content-model work remains after the Philosophy foundation.

### S-051 — 2026-07-22 — Principles Candidate 06

**Context:** Sheldon supplied the browser conversation's full Candidate 06 exploration after using the audit response and prompt verbatim.

**Source SHA-256:** `229E21C6C8E8857C2FD9913A58D59F2EAD9673196EC96082F5E5FFBD5003BC7F`

**Audit:**

1. Candidate 06 is durable, costly, cross-contextual, and sufficiently action-guiding to remain Principle-level.
2. Its independent core is deciding when Spark has sufficient—not perfect—readiness to act given the consequences, safeguards, and practical capacity to respond.
3. The provisional name overweights expansion and implies recoverability that may not exist; `Act With Sufficient Readiness` is a stronger comparison name.
4. Learning may justify action but is not a universal prerequisite; service, obligation, correction, and opportunity may also justify proceeding.
5. Practical response capacity is stronger than theoretical reversibility because operational rollback cannot erase every consequence.
6. Risk distribution, residual harm, cumulative exposure, and repeated temporary strain are important boundaries but should not turn the canonical Principle into a complete risk framework.
7. Candidate 06 remains distinct from Candidate 02's corrigibility and Candidate 05's accountability allocation.
8. Temporary strain versus structural fragility remains a likely Mental Model signal, not another Principle; anti-stagnation remains an anti-gaming boundary.

**Disposition:** Retain Candidate 06 as one Principle candidate centered on sufficient readiness and proceed to Candidate 07. No substantive Sheldon decision is required.

### S-052 — 2026-07-22 — Principles Candidate 07

**Context:** Sheldon supplied the browser conversation's full Candidate 07 exploration after using the audit response and prompt verbatim.

**Source SHA-256:** `6B662791E28D1596735ABA4E33E9E32AA6A4E8F510259A98DEE35C608578BFAE`

3. The provisional name risks implying that Spark can preserve human understanding or guarantee improvement; Spark can preserve supporting conditions and representations only.
4. Candidate 07 remains distinct from Candidate 01: present clarity about a judgment's basis can exist without that basis surviving, while extensive preservation can exist without honest present framing.
5. Historical preservation must remain separate from active authority, current applicability, and truth.
6. Privacy, security, lawful deletion, cognitive load, low consequence, and inexpensive reconstruction appropriately limit preservation.
7. Key-person dependence is a proportional continuity risk, not an automatic violation; specialization and partially tacit judgment remain legitimate.
8. The four source signals form one Principle with boundaries and downstream Governance controls, not multiple preservation Principles.
9. Proposed Mental Models remain signals only and should be tested later rather than accepted during Principles exploration.

**Disposition:** Retain Candidate 07 for comparison. All seven provisional Principle families have now been explored and independently audited; proceed to complete-set comparison before drafting the Principles document. No substantive Sheldon decision is required.

Conduct one focused Challenge round on compressed wording, ordinary-language recognition, collisions, selective use, and hostile gaming; then reassess Close readiness. ### S-069 — 2026-07-27 — Parallel Mental Models opening responses

**Context:** Sheldon supplied independent opening responses to the same Mental Models Exploration 001 prompt from browser ChatGPT and Claude Code. Both read current Spark OS sources without repository mutation.

**Comparison:**

1. Both respect authority, scope, the eight-part Mental Model card, non-authoritative status, and the prohibition on downstream design.
2. The browser response is the stronger exploration artifact: it maps ten candidate families, develops a more complete card, records ownership and glossary boundaries, and supplies a rigorous applied Challenge prompt.
3. The browser response also shows the larger complexity risk. Several families closely track individual Principles, and its preferred `Rollback Is Not Recovery` candidate develops language already explicit in Principle 6.
4. The Claude response is the stronger architectural compression. It reduces the field to three families, explicitly rejects likely Principle or Glossary restatements, and identifies the duplication risk in the rollback candidate earlier.
5. Claude's preferred `Representation Is Not Capability` candidate is more cross-cutting and Manifesto-rooted, but its name is narrower than its core idea, which also covers truth, currency, authority, and safety. Its client example also overstates conversation as the “real indicator”; conversation supplies additional evidence but cannot guarantee comprehension.
6. Neither candidate should yet be designated Candidate 01. A focused comparison should test whether either provides a distinct reusable reasoning mechanism beyond the accepted Principles and whether its name matches its actual scope.

**Disposition:** Use the browser conversation as the primary exploratory workbench because it produces richer auditable source material. Use Claude as a periodic independent compression and duplication challenger, not as a second parallel author for every candidate. Before Candidate 01 proceeds, run one convergence challenge comparing `Rollback Is Not Recovery` with `Representation Is Not Capability`, incorporating Claude's compression findings and correcting the scope issues in its card.

### S-070 — 2026-07-27 — Mental Models convergence challenge

**Result:** The focused browser challenge retains a narrowed `Representation Is Not Capability` and reallocates `Rollback Is Not Recovery` as a Principle 6 application rather than a standalone Mental Model. It rejects a generic merged proxy-versus-reality model.

**Audit:**

1. The response resolves the disagreement through applied comparison rather than preserving both candidates by default.
2. It correctly finds that the rollback candidate's normative and diagnostic content is already unusually explicit in Principle 6; current evidence does not justify a duplicate Philosophy card.
3. It preserves the useful reversibility-versus-recoverability distinction as non-authoritative application source material without editing the accepted Principle.
4. It corrects the scope mismatch in `Representation Is Not Capability` by excluding truth, currency, authority, reliability, and safety from the model's definition.
5. It corrects the earlier signature-versus-conversation overstatement and preserves Spark's humility boundary that comprehension cannot be guaranteed.
6. The surviving card supplies a distinct cross-domain inference test: identify the representation, identify the human capability inferred from it, and ask what the representation actually demonstrates.
7. The card remains vulnerable to paternalism, credential skepticism, excessive verification, false inference from observed performance, and ambiguity about whether client understanding naturally fits under “capability.”
8. Its provisional capability definition must not silently become an accepted Knowledge–Understanding–Judgment–Competence ontology.
9. Reallocation to Principle 6 means retaining source material for future examples or downstream teaching, not reopening or editing the accepted Principles document.

**Disposition:** Convergence passes. Designate provisional Mental Models Candidate 01 — `Representation Is Not Capability`, status `Refine`. Challenge it next against legitimate bounded reliance, misleading performance evidence, client autonomy, credential and experience reliance, capability decay, false negatives, and disproportionate verification. Do not proceed to Candidate 02.

### S-071 — 2026-07-27 — Claude review of Mental Models convergence

**Result:** Claude independently supports the browser convergence result, including reallocation of `Rollback Is Not Recovery`, narrowing of `Representation Is Not Capability`, rejection of the signature-versus-conversation substitution, and continuation without a Sheldon decision.

**Additional findings:**

1. The agreement on rollback is genuine independent convergence: Claude had already classified the family as likely Principle 6 duplication before the browser's applied challenge reached the same result.
2. Claude correctly recognizes that its original Candidate B bundled truth, currency and authority, reliability and safety, and human capability under one intuition despite those questions having different existing owners.
3. Claude correctly identifies the signature example as a substantive epistemic error rather than a wording issue: replacing one proxy with another would violate the candidate's own purpose and Spark's humility boundary.
4. `Presence does not establish current authority` remains a real parked reasoning pattern. It should not disappear into allocation, but it earns later Mental Model exploration only if it adds a reusable interpretive mechanism beyond existing Governance, repository authority, Principle 7, and Glossary distinctions.
5. The narrowed candidate's exclusions—especially output reliability in AI cases—must be stress-tested in application. A written Limits section is not self-enforcing.
6. Treating rollback as an “activation question under Principle 6” does not authorize modification of the accepted Principles document. It remains non-authoritative application source material unless a future governed change is separately justified.

**Disposition:** Claude's response strengthens the current direction and confirms the recommended role split. Do not start a second Claude candidate sequence. Preserve Claude for complete-set compression and other architecture checkpoints. Await the browser's focused Candidate 01 Challenge.

### S-072 — 2026-07-27 — Candidate 01 applied Challenge

**Candidate:** `Representation Is Not Capability`

**Disposition supplied:** `Refine`

**Audit:**

1. The candidate survives the hard boundary cases and establishes a useful proportional inference mechanism: infer no more capability than the evidence reasonably supports and seek no more evidence than the purpose and consequences reasonably require.
2. The response protects legitimate bounded reliance, credentials, external requirements, supported and AI-assisted work, repeated representative performance, client autonomy, accommodation, and low-consequence non-application.
3. It avoids treating independent or unaided performance as the only real form of capability.
4. It distinguishes capability evidence from permission and authority to act.
5. It keeps the Knowledge–Understanding–Judgment–Competence relationship provisional and avoids designing a formal assessment system.
6. Its strongest canonical objection remains real: the Manifesto and Principles already contain most individual applications, so permanent inclusion depends on the cross-domain inference mechanism adding practical reuse.
7. The main terminology defect is now exposed. Observed performance is evidence of capability but is not naturally a “representation.” Broadening the working definition of representation to include observed evidence would distort ordinary language to save the candidate name.
8. The candidate currently operates across three objects that must remain distinct: representations or indicators, broader capability evidence, and the human capability being inferred.
9. `Supported capability` is useful challenge language but should remain a limit or example unless repeated use establishes a genuine Glossary need.
10. The lengthy Challenge source is proportionate for testing but must not transfer into the eventual canonical card.

**Disposition:** Challenge passes; Candidate 01 remains `Refine` and enters formal Audit. Audit must determine conceptual independence, whether client understanding and employee competence remain coherent under one model, the representation/evidence terminology, appropriate name and scope, duplication with accepted Philosophy, and the smallest canonical form. Do not proceed to Candidate 02.

### S-073 — 2026-07-27 — Candidate 01 formal Audit

**Audit disposition supplied:** `Narrow`

**Lifecycle recommendation supplied:** Ready to Close as non-authoritative source material.

**Independent audit:**

1. The formal Audit correctly identifies the surviving mechanism as a representation boundary rather than a general evidence-sufficiency system.
2. It correctly excludes the broader proportional-inference formulation from the model's core because that formulation behaves like a decision rule already governed by Principles 1, 5, and 6.
3. It preserves ordinary terminology: observed performance remains evidence, not a representation; `indicator`, `capability evidence`, `representation substitution`, and `supported capability` are not promoted into permanent vocabulary.
4. One model can cover employee capability and client understanding only at the artifact-versus-human-state level. Operational use should speak about client understanding rather than label clients capable or incapable.
5. The candidate adds a genuine cross-domain diagnostic move beyond its existing domain examples: identify the artifact, identify the human capability attributed because of it, and examine what the artifact does and does not show.
6. The materially compressed eight-part card is proportionate and preserves the necessary limits.
7. The institutional-memory example remains slightly duplication-vulnerable because Principle 7 already handles it directly; retain it only if complete-set comparison shows it demonstrates cross-domain transfer rather than padding.
8. The candidate's practical value remains inferred rather than observed. That is compatible with closure as tested source material but remains a valid reason for later complete-set rejection.

**Disposition:** Accept `Narrow`. Close Candidate 01 as non-authoritative source material using the compressed card and recorded boundaries. Candidate 01 remains eligible for complete-set comparison; it is not accepted or canonical. After concise closure, return to the remaining family inventory and select Candidate 02 based on cross-Principle reasoning value and independence from existing Principle rationale.

### S-074 — 2026-07-27 — Candidate 01 Close and Candidate 02 Explore

**Candidate 01:** Closure passes. `Representation Is Not Capability` is explicitly closed as non-authoritative source material, retains the audited narrow boundary and compressed card, remains eligible for complete-set comparison, and creates no authority or downstream framework.

**Candidate-family compression audit:**

1. The remaining inventory is credibly compressed to five families rather than extended.
2. Influence Pathways, Accountability as Operating Conditions, and Bounded Action Under Consequence are correctly reallocated as direct applications of Principles 4, 5, and 6.
3. Judgment Basis and Revision is correctly held back because its possible mechanism remains too close to Principles 1 and 2 and risks becoming a taxonomy or documentation template.
4. Presence versus current authority remains visibly parked rather than absorbed or discarded.
5. Material Meaning and Future Interpretation is the only remaining family with a plausible cross-Principle transformation lens, but the response correctly narrows Candidate 02 to material meaning under simplification or transfer.

**Candidate 02:** `What Changes When We Simplify?`, status `Refine`, entering Challenge.

**Audit findings:**

1. The proposed diagnostic shift—from amount of detail removed to effect on responsible interpretation or action—is coherent and reusable.
2. Its duplication burden is unusually high because Principle 1 already states that simplicity becomes misleading when it removes information necessary for responsible judgment.
3. Cross-domain application does not by itself establish independence. The Challenge must show a reusable transformation comparison not already completed by Principles 1, 3, and 7.
4. “Simplification” may be narrower than the proposed scope, which also includes translation, aggregation, handoff, preservation, and retirement. The model must not broaden merely to keep all examples.
5. The candidate must remain purpose- and audience-relative: a representation may preserve material meaning for one use while becoming misleading for another.
6. Linked or recoverable context, progressive disclosure, and intentional omission may preserve responsible use without requiring all detail in the immediate representation.
7. The institutional-memory and current-authority cases must not pull Governance and Principle 7 concerns back into the candidate.

**Disposition:** Candidate 01 closure accepted. Candidate 02 selection is defensible and its provisional card is fit for focused Challenge. Use the supplied Challenge prompt with explicit comparison to Principle 1's existing misleading-simplicity boundary and do not proceed to Candidate 03.

### S-075 — 2026-07-27 — Candidate 02 focused Challenge

**Candidate:** `What Changes When We Simplify?`

**Challenge disposition supplied:** `Narrow`

**Independent audit:**

1. Candidate 02 survives Challenge through three interpretive moves beyond Principle 1's normative boundary: before-and-after visibility shift, purpose-relative fidelity, and immediate versus responsibly recoverable context.
2. The response demonstrates that simplification may change prominence, grouping, apparent certainty, and practical visibility even when raw information technically remains available.
3. It protects beneficial simplification and progressive disclosure rather than treating omission or reduced detail as automatic loss.
4. It correctly narrows the coherent family to purpose-relative simplification through removal, summarization, aggregation, restructuring, or audience-directed plain-language treatment.
5. General preservation, retirement, repository presence, current authority, uncaptured handoff knowledge, and preservation metadata are correctly removed.
6. AI remains an example of transformation, not a separate mechanism.
7. The candidate remains highly duplication-vulnerable because Principle 1 already supplies the complete normative rule, while Principles 3 and 7 fully govern some prominent examples.
8. Its main classification risk is becoming an editorial or communication-review method. Formal Audit must determine whether the transformation comparison changes interpretation broadly enough to remain Philosophy.
9. The model's user remains somewhat implicit. Audit should test whether it is useful to creators, reviewers, and decision-makers, and whether dependence on access to an original or “full” source makes it a review procedure.
10. The source representation may itself be incomplete or misleading; before-and-after comparison must not silently treat the source as ground truth.

**Disposition:** Accept `Narrow`. Candidate 02 enters formal Audit using the browser's proposed prompt, supplemented by explicit tests of the model's user, source availability, and the possibility that the source itself is defective. Do not proceed to Candidate 03.

### S-076 — 2026-07-27 — Candidate 02 formal Audit

**Candidate:** `What Changes When We Simplify?`

**Audit disposition supplied:** `Retain`

**Lifecycle recommendation supplied:** Ready to Close as non-authoritative source material.

**Independent audit:**

1. The candidate survives as a distinct transformation lens rather than an additional normative rule.
2. Its four durable comparisons—visibility shift, audience and purpose, layered access, and positive transformation—add enough interpretive value beyond Principle 1 for continued consideration.
3. The model can be activated by creators, reviewers, or recipients and does not depend on formal review, line-by-line comparison, or one authoritative full source.
4. The defective-source test correctly prevents faithful transformation from being mistaken for truth, currency, authority, reliability, legality, or safety.
5. Positive transformation is essential: the model helps remove noise and expose material meaning as well as detect loss.
6. Scope remains bounded to purpose-relative simplification through summary, aggregation, restructuring, removal, or audience-directed explanation.
7. General preservation, retirement, authority, missing knowledge, metadata, and downstream information design remain excluded.
8. The compressed eight-part card is proportionate and the name remains accurate enough for working use.
9. Its principal complete-set vulnerability remains whether it is practically distinguishable from good editorial or information-design practice.
10. Principle 7's relationship and the institutional-memory-adjacent examples should be reconsidered during set comparison if they do not add independent value.

**Disposition:** Accept `Retain`. Close Candidate 02 as non-authoritative source material using the audited compressed card. It remains eligible for complete-set comparison and is neither accepted nor canonical. Before selecting Candidate 03, compare the two held-back cross-Principle families—Judgment Basis and Revision, and Presence versus Material Effect—and explicitly allow the exploration to conclude with only two candidates if neither adds a distinct mechanism.

### S-081 — 2026-07-28 — Prototype-course storage and cross-platform review path

**Question:** Should the prototype course be uploaded only into this audit conversation, or preserved through a GitHub path that supports review across AI platforms?

**Recommendation:** Use the repository Workbench as the durable source and chat uploads only as a transport fallback.

**Proposed non-authoritative location:**

`00-workbench/drafts/academy-prototypes/<prototype-id>/`

**Minimum review packet:**

1. `README.md` — artifact ID, non-authoritative status, intended Academy destination, scope, authoring context, current revision, dependencies, and unresolved questions.
2. `course.md` or a small `modules/` set — the prototype itself.
3. `source-map.md` — accepted sources used, material transformations, and important omissions or unresolved source gaps.
4. `review-brief.md` — the fixed questions and boundaries every reviewer should apply.

**Cross-platform routine:**

1. Freeze the packet in one commit on a dedicated prototype branch.
2. Give each reviewer the same commit or exact exported files.
3. Reviewers return findings without editing the review revision.
4. Reconcile material findings in this foundational audit.
5. Apply agreed revisions in a later commit and review the delta.
6. Merge the prototype into the Workbench only if preserving it has continuing value; graduate content to `04-academy/` only after its destination, source authority, and acceptance are clear.

**Complexity boundary:** Do not create a general AI-review subsystem, per-platform artifact copies, or competing review authorities. Raw AI responses need not be committed unless their full wording has durable evidentiary value. The reconciled audit should preserve the material findings and decisions.

**Mental Models boundary:** The course remains an Academy prototype. Incidental observations may become later Mental Model evidence, but the course must not be redesigned primarily to validate the closed candidates.

**Disposition:** Repository-first, revision-specific cross-review is preferable to chat-only storage. A chat attachment is useful for immediate inspection but is not the durable shared source.

### S-085 — 2026-07-28 — First independent substantive course audit

**Attachment integrity finding:** The two files supplied as the Claude and browser reviews are byte-for-byte identical. Both contain 770 lines and have SHA-256 `538F9F16AE1CDF04C7A4EAE2EE111CEBC3A1D0BA0BAD6A11E361A461BAF2748E`. Only one distinct independent review is therefore available.

**Review disposition supplied:** Advance to bounded SME/Canon sourcing while retaining PR #12 as a draft, unmerged Workbench artifact.

**Strongly supported findings:**

1. The prototype's central weakness is the unverified scope and meaning of its three-category taxonomy, not merely missing numeric thresholds.
2. The Learn section labels assumptions, but cases, feedback, and fixed answer keys progressively present derived domain claims with greater certainty.
3. The Source Map does not adequately separate fictional case facts from the domain propositions used to judge those cases.
4. Broad hard-gate language about refinance and non-owner-occupied properties is unsafe without a defined taxonomy and product scope.
5. Current official CMHC material confirms insured products for qualifying non-owner-occupied two-to-four-unit rental properties and a qualifying refinance product for adding secondary suites. This contradicts universal exclusion language while not necessarily resolving the prototype's specific single-unit rental or debt-consolidation cases.
6. The verify-versus-escalate boundary remains unresolved and must not be graded as though one route has been operationally accepted.
7. The Knowledge Check includes constrained application items despite describing itself as limited to Know and Understand.
8. The Markdown and HTML materially correspond but diverge in source-map completeness, warning persistence, and assessment caveats.
9. Claims about the most common learner error or highest-value distinction are authoring hypotheses, not observed Spark evidence.
10. The assessment quantity and proposed downstream infrastructure are appropriate subjects for proportionality review after domain validity is established.
11. The prototype correctly preserves the distinction between course completion, assessment performance, and demonstrated competence.

**Severity refinements:**

1. The unverified taxonomy is a Blocker to learner use or Academy advancement, not a blocker to preserving the completed Workbench experiment.
2. Mock-up warning placement is a Material pre-use gate. The current mock-up was never authorized as learner-facing content.
3. Assessment volume is Advisory at prototype stage because the vertical slice was intended to expose design problems; it becomes a revision concern only if the artifact continues toward use.

**Comparison with Claude's author self-review:**

**Agreement:**

- domain content and numbers require SME validation;
- the taxonomy itself is unverified;
- escalation is unresolved;
- the course does not establish competence;
- and skimming can create misuse risk.

**New independent value:**

- primary-source evidence against broad exclusion language;
- derived-answer authority analysis;
- Source Map misclassification;
- Knowledge Check level inconsistency;
- mock-up/Markdown warning drift;
- unsupported learner-error ranking;
- and explicit proportionality concerns.

**Questions-to-owner refinement:**

The review lists eight questions for Sheldon, but not all are human preference decisions. Current facts, insurer scope, regulatory requirements, and source selection should first be developed through authoritative evidence and licensed SME review. Sheldon should be asked to choose organizational purpose, intended learner scope, and the desired role of the prototype after those options are made concrete.

**Process disposition:**

1. Do not run the report's full reconciliation prompt yet.
2. Obtain the missing distinct second review.
3. Reconcile cross-platform findings here.
4. If the second review introduces no material objection, use Course Prototype 001 as a bounded first Canon-to-Academy sourcing exercise.
5. Begin with a domain-definition and claim-inventory brief, not a full Canon architecture, Governance cadence, or prototype rewrite.
6. Keep PR #12 frozen, draft, and unmerged throughout.

**Mental Models:** The report supplies a small applied observation for `What Changes When We Simplify?` and continued alignment evidence for `Representation Is Not Capability`, but no repeated-use evidence sufficient to reopen Mental Models Exploration 001.

### S-086 — 2026-07-28 — Cross-platform reconciliation of Course Prototype 001

**Distinct second review verified:** 124 lines, SHA-256 `4413EA152F897F5AB9C8EB4ABA38158CB4706B804B27F2CF9C57C3C42D32D750`.

**Second-review disposition:** Revise three concrete Workbench defects, then advance to SME/Canon sourcing.

**Convergence:**

1. PR #12 is the correct frozen review object and must remain draft and unmerged.
2. The prototype is a useful, unusually transparent vertical slice rather than accepted course content.
3. The non-owner-occupied exclusion is materially overbroad because current CMHC insurance exists for qualifying non-owner-occupied two-to-four-unit rental properties.
4. The mock-up Source Map omits the Knowledge Check row present in Markdown.
5. The general taxonomy, numeric thresholds, terminology, and cases require source-backed SME review.
6. Completion and Knowledge Check performance are not represented as competence.
7. The HTML and Markdown materially correspond but require coordinated revision.
8. No Governance, synchronization, or structured-data infrastructure should be created from one prototype.

**Material disagreements resolved against the artifact:**

1. **Taxonomy risk:** The longer browser report is stronger. The artifact itself states that the general three-category conceptual structure is unverified, yet uses the taxonomy to generate categorical feedback and fixed answer keys. This is a Blocker to learner use or Academy advancement, though not to Workbench preservation.
2. **Source Map adequacy:** The longer report is stronger. The Markdown map is transparent but not fully adequate: it classifies Knowledge Check facts, figures, and correct answers as fictional scenario content even though the correct answers are derived domain claims whose authority depends on the unverified taxonomy. It also does not separately map pricing, program-availability, documentation, learner-error, or escalation claims.
3. **Knowledge Check level:** The longer report is clearly correct. Items 5–11 require constrained application to new scenarios; `prototype-findings.md` explicitly admits several Apply-level items; and the facilitator note states that a high score shows application of taught patterns. The instrument must not claim Know/Understand only.
4. **Warnings and learner safety:** The longer report is stronger as a pre-use assessment. Opening warnings are prominent, but categorical answer surfaces are not locally bounded. Because the artifact is not currently learner-facing, this is a Material pre-use gate rather than an immediate preservation failure.
5. **Over-complexity:** Neither extreme should control. The packet is proportionate as a vertical-slice experiment, while twenty-two practice and assessment items are likely excessive if the lesson advances toward actual use. Defer trimming until the domain foundation is settled.
6. **Confirmed contradiction:** Describe the occupancy issue as a confirmed scope contradiction or material overgeneralization. It disproves the universal wording but does not by itself classify the single-unit rental case. The secondary-suite refinance product similarly defeats a universal refinance exclusion, while not necessarily changing the debt-consolidation refinance case.

**Concrete Claude findings retained:**

1. Add the omitted Knowledge Check row or one authoritative Source Map reference in the future HTML revision.
2. Record the authored mock-up SHA-256 or soften the corresponding verification claim.
3. Narrow or qualify the non-owner-occupied case and answer after the domain model is sourced.

**Sequence correction:**

Do not apply those three fixes piecemeal before sourcing. A narrow case edit could preserve a taxonomy that later evidence changes more substantially. First define the classification purpose and build a bounded claim inventory; then revise Markdown, findings, assessments, Source Map, and HTML together.

**Reconciled disposition:** Retain PR #12 as the frozen evidence of Prototype 001. Advance to a bounded domain-definition and claim-inventory exercise, followed by primary-source and licensed-SME review. Do not begin full Canon architecture, Governance changes, or Academy installation.

**Reconciled priorities:**

1. Define what the proposed categories are intended to classify.
2. Inventory the minimum domain claims on which the prototype depends.
3. Use primary official sources to test the taxonomy's viable scope and identify contradictions or insurer-specific exceptions.
4. Present a small set of source-supported taxonomy options and operational consequences for Sheldon's subject-matter direction.
5. Obtain licensed-current-practitioner review.
6. Only then revise the full prototype packet coherently.
7. Re-audit the revised frozen revision before any merge or Academy candidate.

**Mental Models:** The occupancy overgeneralization is a concrete applied example of simplification hiding material scope, and the completion boundary remains a clean example of representation not establishing capability. These remain incidental observations and do not reopen Mental Models Exploration 001.

### S-094 — 2026-08-06 — Course Prototype 001 sourcing exercise initiated

**Selected next priority:** Bounded domain-definition and claim-inventory research for Course Prototype 001.

**Frozen review object:** PR #12 remains open, draft, and unmerged at preservation commit `d277d71351ae95c7c47d168860fb8937c7a78482`. Its seven-file packet is not to be edited during research.

**Problem definition:** The sourcing gap is not limited to missing numeric thresholds. The prototype's general three-category framing, definitions, claims about insurance cost and lender treatment, universal or near-universal occupancy and refinance exclusions, practical consequences, five scenario classifications, assessment rationales, and twelve answer keys all depend on an unverified domain model.

**Research objective:** Determine what `insured`, `insurable`, and `uninsurable` can defensibly classify; separate federal high-/low-ratio requirements, borrower-paid transactional insurance, lender-obtained low-ratio or portfolio/bulk insurance, insurer eligibility, lender overlays, and possible Spark operational shorthand; inventory every material prototype claim; and identify the smallest set of evidence and licensed-practitioner decisions needed before revision.

**Boundaries:**

1. Use current primary official sources and record exact source dates and scope.
2. Do not treat the proposed taxonomy as canonical or force it to survive.
3. Do not rewrite the prototype, fill placeholders, create Canon, design schemas, alter Governance, install Academy content, or edit GitHub.
4. Separate externally researchable facts from organizational choices and licensed-SME judgment.
5. Keep source currency, structured-data infrastructure, review cadence, and full course architecture parked unless directly necessary to describe a claim risk.

**Role separation:** A separate browser research task should produce the brief. This Codex conversation remains the independent audit and reconciliation authority. A second cross-platform review may follow after the primary brief exists; duplicate parallel drafting is not required at the first step.

## Next Intake

### S-095 — 2026-08-06 — Independent audit of Course Prototype 001 Domain Definition and Claim Inventory

**Artifact received:** Complete 499-line non-authoritative research brief covering classification purpose, terminology, 55 material claims, boundary cases, framing options, minimum evidence, full prototype impact, human decisions, 31-source ledger, source gaps, and recommended practitioner review.

**Disposition:** Refine. The central conclusion is well supported: the prototype's insured / insurable / uninsurable framing does not survive as an exhaustive general taxonomy, while its narrower warning that LTV alone cannot determine insurance eligibility or operational treatment remains useful.

**Independently confirmed findings:**

1. Current CMHC, Sagen, Canada Guaranty, and federal materials establish purpose-limited insured secondary-suite refinancing, defeating a universal refinance hard gate.
2. Current CMHC Income Property, Sagen Investment Property, and Canada Guaranty Rental Advantage materials establish mortgage-insurance products for qualifying non-owner-occupied two-to-four-unit purchases, defeating a universal rental-property exclusion.
3. Current federal and insurer materials expressly contemplate owner occupancy of one unit within qualifying multi-unit properties.
4. Published insurer eligibility does not prove lender availability, actual approval, pricing, documentation, or Spark-accessible workflow.
5. The brief correctly separates researchable external facts, licensed-practitioner operational findings, and genuine Spark choices.
6. It stays out of Canon, Academy installation, Governance redesign, schemas, semantic CI, and prototype rewriting.

**Required refinements:**

1. **Case 4 disposition:** The frozen scenario omits unit count. The universal rationale that non-owner occupancy independently disqualifies every file is contradicted, but the specific case is underdetermined rather than affirmatively shown insurable. Revise C-26, C-44, the impact map, and executive wording accordingly. Two-to-four-unit products cannot classify an unspecified or one-unit rental case.
2. **Refinance precision:** Existence of secondary-suite exceptions disproves the prototype's hard-gate answer keys and `regardless of equity` reasoning. It does not by itself disprove the looser empirical phrase `generally excluded`; characterize that broader phrase as unsupported or overbroad unless prevalence evidence exists.
3. **Source completeness:** Add the direct CMHC Income Property and Sagen Investment Property pages to the ledger. They are central evidence and should not be represented only by product hubs.
4. **Claim traceability:** Replace generic source labels in material claim rows with explicit source-ledger IDs, at least for Critical and High claims, so each disposition can be reproduced.
5. **Framing comparison:** Options 1–3 are not mutually exclusive. Separate dimensions may be taught through a decision sequence and could coexist with tightly scoped shorthand. Option 4, abandoning the content framing while retaining the process experiment, remains credible and should not be dropped without evidence.
6. **Proportional evidence gates:** Not all thirteen listed items are prerequisites to a coherent Workbench correction. Separate facts required before any factual rewrite from lender-panel, escalation, pricing, documentation, and learner-behaviour evidence required only before learner-facing or operational use.
7. **Self-employment precision:** A two-year requirement from one named Canada Guaranty product does not establish a universal qualification issue. Keep the point explicitly program-specific.
8. **Practitioner-review safety:** Current lender policies, rate sheets, submission portals, and insurer guides may be restricted or confidential. The next reviewer must not publish credentials, client data, proprietary documents, or access-controlled content. Record summarized findings, source owner, effective date, and access limitation without copying restricted material into a public repository.

**Strengths retained:**

1. Clear non-authoritative status and frozen-PR boundary.
2. Strong separation of LTV status, actual insurance, insurance channel, eligibility, premium responsibility, insurer/product, lender overlay, and operational treatment.
3. Comprehensive prototype impact map rather than piecemeal corrections.
4. Honest source gaps and refusal to ask Sheldon for externally researchable facts.
5. Proportionate licensed-practitioner next review after the evidence layer.

**Process decision:** Request a focused correction addendum rather than a complete 499-line rewrite. After the addendum, commission one independent cross-platform compression/source review. Only then ask Sheldon to choose the learner-facing purpose and framing, followed by a licensed-practitioner operational check. PR #12 remains frozen, draft, and unmerged.

## Next Intake

**Audit observations:**

1. The addendum does not revise PR #12, draft course content, create Canon, alter Governance, or initiate practitioner review.

### S-097 — 2026-08-08 — Reconciliation of Claude domain-brief review

**Review received:** `Independent Compression and Source Review — Course Prototype 001 Domain Brief`, disposition `Refine`.

**Verification quality:** Claude re-fetched PR #12 at preservation commit `d277d71351ae95c7c47d168860fb8937c7a78482`, confirmed the two preserved source hashes from Git objects, verified the frozen Case 3–5 wording, and spot-checked current federal, CMHC, and Canada Guaranty sources. The reported LF/CRLF checkout difference was correctly identified as cosmetic rather than content drift.

**Convergence:**

1. The three-label framing is not an exhaustive national taxonomy.
2. LTV, insurance status, eligibility, channel, premium responsibility, insurer/product, lender overlay, and operational treatment must remain distinguishable.
3. Purpose-limited secondary-suite refinancing defeats a universal refinance hard gate without establishing ordinary debt-consolidation eligibility or market prevalence.
4. Case 5's owner-occupied duplex satisfies the federal high-ratio occupancy structure when one unit is occupied by the borrower, subject to the other applicable criteria.
5. The self-employment observation remains product-specific.
6. Insurer advertising does not establish lender or Spark-panel availability.
7. Shorthand, dimensions, and a decision sequence may be complementary; abandonment remains credible.
8. The research depth was proportionate because it exposed multiple Critical errors rather than merely adding nuance.

**Disagreement 1 — Case 4 probability inference: rejected.** Claude proposed that the case's singular phrase `rental/investment property` makes a one-unit property the more probable reading and therefore makes the original `Uninsurable` answer more likely correct. A duplex, triplex, or fourplex is also one property. The frozen scenario supplies no unit-count evidence, and Spark's accepted reasoning discipline does not permit ordinary-language intuition to manufacture a consequential mortgage fact. Retain the corrected disposition: the scenario is underdetermined; the universal occupancy rationale is contradicted. If later confirmed as one unit, the reviewed products and federal low-ratio CMHC criteria would support a narrower finding that no identified insured pathway was established, not a universal actor-free definition of `uninsurable`.

**Statutory precision retained:** Current Insurable Housing Loan Regulations define eligible residential property as one-to-four units; section 5 requires borrower or qualifying-relative occupancy of at least one unit for high-ratio loans, while section 6(1)(l) imposes the occupancy criterion on low-ratio loans only when the property contains one unit. This legal mechanism should be added if the research packet is ever preserved, but is not needed to reopen it.

**Disagreement 2 — assessment gate: accepted with preservation boundary.** Gate A should include identifying, deprecating, or removing known unsupported categorical answer keys in any successor Workbench correction. Gate B should govern authoring and validating replacement learner-facing assessments, especially where Spark judgment or escalation thresholds are involved. This does not authorize editing frozen PR #12; the preserved prototype remains evidence of the original experiment.

**Final research disposition:** Accept the original brief plus correction addendum and this reconciliation as non-authoritative working source. No further browser or Claude revision loop is proportionate. The remaining nested-link formatting and statutory citation precision are preservation-time advisories only.

**Decisions now genuinely requiring Sheldon:**

1. Whether the topic should continue using the three labels as explicit Spark shorthand, proceed through separate dimensions and a decision sequence without relying on the taxonomy, or remain only a completed process experiment.
2. Whether to commission the bounded licensed-practitioner review after selecting the intended learner-facing purpose.

**Deferred decision:** Exact escalation thresholds should not be chosen abstractly. They depend on learner role, delegated authority, accessible verification sources, consequence, and practitioner-confirmed workflow.

**Human authorization:** Sheldon directed this audit task to preserve the review state, practitioner-review handoff, and lessons learned within the existing PR #12 Workbench branch, then return primary attention to foundational Spark OS development.

2. `domain-review-record.md` — corrected domain findings, source map, decision dimensions, candidate sequence, and Gate A / Gate B distinction.
3. `practitioner-review-brief.md` — bounded instructions for a current licensed practitioner.
4. `prototype-lessons.md` — durable implications for future Spark OS, Canon, Academy, and course-development work.

3. No accepted Philosophy, Governance, Canon, Academy, Brain, or existing Workbench file entered the commit.
4. Repository integrity validation and staged-diff validation passed locally.

**Durable prototype lessons:**

1. A bounded prototype may precede complete architecture when its non-authoritative status and promotion boundary are explicit.
2. Canon must establish a domain's evidence basis and authority before Academy treats related content as instruction.
3. Answer keys, explanations, examples, and feedback are substantive claims and require the same evidence discipline as lesson prose.
4. Simplification must be tested for materially changed meaning, not merely readability.
5. A knowledge representation, completed course, acknowledgement, or correct output does not demonstrate understanding, judgment, or competence.
6. External facts, licensed-practitioner operational findings, Spark policy choices, and unresolved matters must remain distinguishable.
7. Gate A covers correction or deprecation of known unsupported material; Gate B governs validated replacement learner-facing or operational content.
8. Failed or superseded prototypes should be preserved as evidence and replaced by successor artifacts rather than silently rewritten.
9. Independent roles for exploration, domain research, audit, practitioner review, approval, installation, and verification improve error detection when their authority remains explicit.
10. New infrastructure must earn its place through recurring need; this prototype does not justify premature Canon schemas, Academy machinery, or semantic automation.

**Return-to-foundations priority:**

1. Resume Mental Models at an evidence checkpoint rather than reopening the course prototype.
2. Decide whether `Representation Is Not Capability` and `What Changes When We Simplify?` should be distilled, observed further, or reclassified.
3. Resume the foundational Glossary after the Mental Models decision so definitions support rather than replace the other Philosophy documents.
4. Complete the remaining five-document Philosophy foundation before broad Canon or Academy infrastructure is designed.
5. After the Philosophy foundation is stable, define only the minimum Canon sourcing and Academy derivation standards demonstrated necessary by the prototype.

- That boundary was already explicit in accepted Philosophy and the prototype instructions.

**Candidate 02 — What Changes When We Simplify?:**

- The prototype supplied strong single-case evidence that the transformation lens can expose material meaning loss.
- The three-label mortgage taxonomy hid independent dimensions and produced overbroad answer keys; the review changed the successor direction toward nine dimensions and a decision sequence.

- It strengthens the prior Academy/editorial ownership concern more than it demonstrates recurring foundational-Philosophy use.
- **Disposition:** Provisionally reclassify downstream as non-authoritative source material for a future Academy or content-transformation design standard. Do not install it downstream yet.

**Set disposition:** No Distill, no accepted Mental Model, no Candidate 03, no Philosophy modification, and no repository installation. The accepted Mental Models scaffold remains intentionally empty of accepted model cards.

**Lineage-bias result:** The prototype was selected partly because the two representation-themed candidates could apply. It cannot establish their dominance. A separate category-collapse signal was observed but remains better handled through Canon definitions, decision dimensions, and accepted Principles than through a manufactured third model.

1. Deferring `Knowledge` and `Understanding` correctly avoids reopening the closed ontology problem.
2. Keeping mortgage and instructional terminology outside the foundational Glossary preserves the Canon and Academy boundaries.
3. Treating repository-status terms as Governance-owned avoids duplicate authority, while leaving room for later cross-reference entries.
4. Leaving `Trust` in ordinary language is proportionate because current accepted duties do not depend on one technical theory of trust.
5. The three candidate families expose genuine cross-document collisions without asserting a ladder or complete ontology.
6. No Sheldon decision is needed before Challenge; both reserved decisions can be narrowed through evidence first.

**Required Challenge refinements:**

1. `Material` already has accepted definitional wording in `values.md`. The Glossary must preserve and cross-reference that authority rather than create a subtly different second definition.
2. The proposed shared `Authority` definition may force two related but distinct senses into one abstraction. Challenge decision authority and repository/source authority separately, then test whether a genuinely useful shared core remains.
3. `Accountability` currently includes duties to explain, review, and respond, while `Responsibility` lists multiple required actions. Challenge whether those are definitions, contextual examples, or newly introduced obligations.
4. `Evidence` must be relational to a claim, question, judgment, or decision and remain distinct from source, fact, assertion, proof, authority, rationale, and the wider basis of judgment.
5. `Judgment` risks absorbing belief, reasoning, recommendation, decision, and action into one term. Challenge the smallest meaning that faithfully interprets accepted Philosophy without treating all discretion as professional judgment.
6. `Capability`, `Competence`, and `Readiness` have a credible distinction but must survive cases involving supported performance, team capability, stale competence, licensing, supervision, temporary conditions, and adequate readiness without complete independent competence.
7. The claimed smallest set needs an omission test. Compare `proportionate`, `uncertainty`, `basis of judgment`, and `consequence` against the nine candidates; add or substitute nothing unless an omitted term presents a stronger material collision.
8. Each entry must be tested for circularity and hidden dependency on another unsettled candidate definition.

**Authority decision boundary:** Do not ask Sheldon yet whether `Authority` receives one shared definition. Challenge should first determine whether a shared definition clarifies both uses or merely hides an important distinction. Present a human choice only if two coherent architectures remain after testing.

**Professional-judgment boundary:** Do not create a separate entry merely because the phrase recurs. First test whether a bounded distinction under `Judgment`, with professional scope left to external obligations and domain Canon, is sufficient.

**Next phase:** Focused Challenge of the nine provisional records, including cluster-level collision tests and the omission test. Do not draft the complete candidate Glossary.

### S-106 — 2026-08-08 — Final focused Glossary Challenge on Proportionate

**Human direction:** Sheldon authorized one final Challenge limited to `Proportionate`. The other candidates, formal Audit, Distill, and canonical drafting remained out of scope.

**Artifact updated:** `00-workbench/drafts/foundational-glossary-exploration-001.md` now contains the completed accepted-passage review, boundary cases, neighbouring-term distinctions, circularity findings, misuse analysis, disposition, post-Challenge set, and exact formal-Audit prompt.

**Source integrity:** Accepted Principle citations use the preserved approved source with SHA-256 `398DCEA61EFC2C5F9F6B67C3D08186CFFEC76E4B8E01E5391811FA35557C230C`. The historical local branch was not treated as current repository authority, and no accepted Philosophy was modified.

**Entry-necessity result:** A bounded foundational entry is justified. Across Values and all seven Principles, proportionality consistently shapes the kind or degree of response so Spark neither neglects what matters nor applies maximum process indiscriminately. Navigation-only treatment would leave readers to reconstruct the same two-direction boundary repeatedly.

**Provisional wording:** `Proportionate` means matched in kind and degree to context and what is at stake. It may require escalation or restraint based on available evidence and acknowledged uncertainty, but cannot reduce or override binding legal, regulatory, contractual, ethical, or professional obligations.

**Boundary findings:**

- low probability does not neutralize catastrophic or irreversible consequences;
- cumulative minor effects can justify a stronger response;
- materiality triggers attention but does not require maximum process;
- urgency can justify timely action with bounded preparation and safeguards;
- further review can become disproportionate when it is unlikely to change the decision;
- privacy and confidentiality constrain evidence use rather than disappear through proportionality;
- resource limits may affect form or timing but cannot normalize foreseeable material harm;
- technical reversibility does not erase human, relational, privacy, trust, or reputational consequences;
- and preservation can fail through both excess and insufficiency.

**Circularity result:** `Proportionate` remains distinct from Material, Consequence, Judgment, Accountability, and Readiness. Application requires contextual judgment but is not whatever a decision-maker prefers; the response must be explainably related to accepted evidence, uncertainty, stakes, context, and obligations.

**Disposition:** Retain `Proportionate` as a foundational Glossary candidate. Do not create a formula, score, numerical threshold, equal-balancing rule, or universal decision procedure. Domain thresholds and mandatory controls remain downstream or externally binding.

**Post-Challenge minimum:** Nine substantive definitions—Decision Authority, Accountability, Responsibility, Evidence, Judgment, Capability, Competence, Readiness, and Proportionate—plus one faithful `Material` navigation entry preserving accepted Values wording.

**Readiness:** The set is ready for formal Audit as non-authoritative Workbench source material. This does not authorize Distill, a complete candidate Glossary, installation, or changes to accepted Philosophy.

**Guidance boundary:** No Sheldon decision is required before formal Audit.

### S-107 — 2026-08-08 — Independent audit of final Proportionate Challenge

**Intake:** Independently review the completed focused Challenge on `Proportionate`, confirm whether its disposition and post-Challenge architecture are sound, and preserve any constraints needed for formal Audit.

**Audit disposition:** **Pass with bounded wording questions for formal Audit.** Retaining `Proportionate` as a foundational Glossary candidate is justified. The term performs a recurring cross-document function that ordinary usage and navigation alone would not protect reliably: it shapes both the kind and degree of a response, can require escalation or restraint, and rejects both neglect and indiscriminate maximum process.

**What survived:**

- the proposed entry is distinct from `Material`: materiality identifies potential significance, while proportionality concerns the response;
- it is distinct from `Readiness`: readiness is one application of proportionality, not its definition;
- low probability does not neutralize severe or irreversible consequences;
- cumulative effects and warning signs can change the response required;
- binding obligations remain constraints or minimums rather than factors that proportionality may trade away;
- the term does not create a formula, score, equal-balancing rule, numerical threshold, or universal decision procedure; and
- thresholds, controls, and domain applications remain downstream.

**Formal-Audit wording questions:**

1. The phrase `based on available evidence and acknowledged uncertainty` must not imply that these are the only relevant inputs. Accepted sources also make applicable obligations, rights, authority, context, consequences, and practical constraints relevant. Formal Audit should decide whether `in light of` or another construction preserves this without expanding the definition.
2. `Binding legal, regulatory, contractual, ethical, or professional obligations` must mean obligations that are actually applicable and authoritative in the situation. The Glossary must not decide which ethical or professional standards bind, create a new hierarchy among them, or make every asserted obligation controlling.
3. `What is at stake` is a useful plain-language boundary but cannot become an unbounded invitation to add interests not supported by accepted sources. Application should remain explainable against relevant evidence, uncertainty, context, affected interests, authority, and obligations.

**Set-level result:** The complete post-Challenge set—nine substantive definitions plus one faithful `Material` navigation entry—is ready for formal Audit as non-authoritative source material. Formal Audit must still test the set as a whole for circularity, unnecessary entries, source fidelity, owner collisions, plain-language usability, and hidden creation of duties, thresholds, status rules, hierarchies, ontology, or theory.

**Preserved cautions:** Absence is evidence only where the missing observation, information, or result was reasonably expected under relevant conditions. System capability must not imply human competence, judgment, responsibility, or moral agency. Applicable competence standards remain external or downstream. Repository/source authority remains Governance-owned.

**Guidance boundary:** No Sheldon decision is required. Do not begin Distill, draft a complete candidate Glossary, reopen deferred ontology terms, or modify accepted Philosophy before formal Audit concludes.

- professional judgment remains a bounded application;
- system capability implies no human qualities or moral agency;
- Competence standards remain externally or downstream supplied;
- Readiness remains situational and non-recursive;
- Proportionate retains escalation, restraint, relevant-input, applicable-obligation, no-formula, no-equal-balancing, and no-convenience boundaries;
- Material reproduces the accepted Values definition exactly and remains navigation only; and
- standards, thresholds, controls, criteria, and domain applications remain downstream.

**Self-review result:** Source fidelity, six-field compliance, authority boundaries, downstream ownership, and non-authoritative status pass. No specific defect requires return to formal Audit.

**Independent-audit focus:** Test whether Accountability's definition is natural in daily use without adding duties, and whether Proportionate's distinction can be compressed without losing audited safeguards.

**Canonical effect:** None. `01-philosophy/glossary.md`, accepted Philosophy, and Governance were not modified. Candidate 001 is uninstalled.

**Guidance boundary:** No Sheldon decision is required before independent candidate audit. Explicit approval remains required before acceptance or installation.

### S-113 — 2026-08-09 — Independent audit of Foundational Glossary Candidate 001

**Intake:** Independently audit the distilled ten-entry candidate for source fidelity, six-field compliance, daily usability, conceptual boundaries, authority ownership, and readiness for human approval.

**Candidate fingerprint:** SHA-256 `23131D1F4DAD35069F1781AE335B93806063EE5453DEB0193284CA8770668279` before audit-directed revision.

**Current-source verification:** The independent audit successfully accessed GitHub. Current `main` is `2b467953d84efa631655113549c0353e22b4bc5a`. The Manifesto, Values, Principles, Mental Models scaffold, Glossary scaffold, AI Governance, and repository map at current `main` have blob-identical content to the candidate's cached accepted revision `37fcc16790c88606934e0a4616ae83eaee2f53b6`. The candidate's substantive source basis is therefore current despite the Distill task's truthful access limitation. The candidate should record this later independent verification before approval.

**Structural result:** Pass. The candidate contains exactly ten headings and sixty required fields: nine substantive definition candidates plus one `Material` navigation candidate. All internal related-term anchors resolve. Every status is explicitly proposed and non-authoritative. `Material` exactly reproduces the accepted Values sentence.

**Set-level disposition:** **Refine through one bounded drafting correction; do not return to formal Audit.** The architecture, ownership, definitions, and principal distinctions survive. No term should be added, removed, merged, or reopened.

**Required corrections:**

1. **Source note:** Record that independent audit retrieved current GitHub `main` at `2b467953…` and confirmed the relevant source blobs are identical to `37fcc167…`. Preserve the fact that the original Distill task itself could not refresh sources.
2. **Accountability definition:** Replace the slightly fragmentary `Being identifiable as answerable...` with `Accountability means being identifiable as answerable for a decision, duty, outcome, or operating condition.` This improves daily naturalness without adding a duty.
3. **Capability distinction:** Replace `System capability describes bounded performance` with `For a system, capability describes what it can produce under specified conditions`. Preserve the existing prohibition on implying human competence, judgment, responsibility, accountability, or moral agency. This avoids shifting from capacity to an ambiguous performance-status claim.
4. **Competence distinction:** Replace `Supported participation may be appropriate without complete independent competence` with `Supported participation does not by itself establish complete independent competence.` The candidate should clarify competence, not grant a general permission for participation.
5. **Proportionate distinction:** Remove the awkward and potentially exhaustive `It considers only...` construction. Use concise wording that preserves relevant inputs, escalation or restraint, applicable binding requirements, no source-selection power, no formula or equal-balancing rule, no convenience default, and downstream ownership of specific thresholds.

**Recommended Proportionate distinction:** `A proportionate response may require escalation or restraint in light of relevant evidence, uncertainty, consequences, rights, applicable obligations, legitimate authority, and practical constraints. Proportionality neither determines which sources apply nor overrides an applicable binding requirement. It is not a formula, equal-balancing rule, or permission to default to convenience; specific thresholds remain with applicable authoritative sources.`

**Preserved findings:** Authority remains decision-scoped while repository/source authority remains Governance-owned. Evidence retains the reasonably-expected-absence boundary. Judgment remains evaluative and non-automated. Competence standards remain externally or downstream supplied. Readiness is situational and non-recursive. Material remains exact Values-owned navigation. No wording creates a new ontology, source hierarchy, decision right, universal threshold, or accepted status.

**Next gate:** Produce one revised complete candidate with only the five corrections above, a change summary, current-source verification note, and new SHA-256. Then independently compare the revision against this audit before asking Sheldon for approval. Do not begin acceptance or installation in the revision step.

**Guidance boundary:** No Sheldon decision is required for the bounded revision. Explicit human approval remains required after the revised candidate passes independent re-audit.
